Basics

Account Security at IFTTT

At IFTTT, we believe everything works better together, but some things might be better off separate (a hacker and your passwords, for example). Given the access IFTTT requires, you might ask: Is IFTTT safe?

In this article, we’ll outline some of the ways we ensure your data and connected third-party accounts are safe on IFTTT.

What is IFTTT?

IFTTT is an automation platform with over 1000 apps and devices ready to use in your Applets.

You can set your heating to turn on 10 minutes before you get home from work, or set your smart lights to flash green when Elon Musk tweets about Dogecoin. You may not recognize all the words in that last sentence, but the important thing to remember is that IFTTT needs access to your devices/accounts in order to power these automations.

With great power comes great responsibility, and we don’t take the trust our 30+ million users place in us lightly.

What are third-party services?

Services on IFTTT are applications, devices, and APIs that can be used in Applets and are usually controlled by a third-party. Some examples include Instagram, Phillips Hue, and Telegram.

How Does IFTTT connect to services?

Most services on IFTTT use OAuth, or Open Authorization, which means users can grant IFTTT restricted access to their third-party account without having to give IFTTT their password.

Rather, an access token is provided to IFTTT which grants specific permissions (like making a new post on X on a user’s behalf) without giving IFTTT full control over the account.

How can I revoke access?

To revoke IFTTT’s access to a third-party service, head to ifttt.com/my_services, select a service, then click Remove.

Please note that some services like Facebook Pages and Google also provide their own method for removing third-party access directly through their platforms. Using either method will successfully remove the access token granted to IFTTT

Are logs kept at IFTTT?

Every trigger check, Applet run, and error is logged to a secure database. These logs are kept for 7 days to help monitor system performance, while activity feed information is stored for 30 days to assist with troubleshooting. When monitoring overall system health, we only use datasets that do not include personal information such as trigger details or action data. Activity feed data is separate and only accessed as part of a customer support interaction.

Does IFTTT sell user data?

IFTTT does not sell personal user data to third-parties.

How does IFTTT use customer data?

The limited personal information stored may be used to provide you with better suggested Applets and services that you may be interested. Occasionally, we may aggregate anonymous customer data to gain a better understanding of our user base.

How do I clear my data?

To clear all of your data from IFTTT’s servers, head to ifttt.com/settings and click Delete my account.

Also note that you can request an export of your data from the same page. This will be sent to you via email as a JSON file.

For a more in-depth breakdown of IFTTT’s Data management and privacy measures, view the Privacy Policy Page.

How can I improve the security of my account?

To keep your account secure, we recommend the following steps:

  • Enable two-step verification (2FA): Adding an extra layer of security is easy. Simply sign in to your account, go to Settings, then click Enable Two-Step Verification and follow the on-screen instructions.
  • Avoid reusing passwords: Use a unique password for each account to reduce the risk of unauthorized access.
  • Create a strong password: Choose a password that’s at least 8 characters long and includes a mix of upper and lower case letters, numbers, and special characters.

Common errors and troubleshooting tips

About troubleshooting

We’ll provide some basic troubleshooting steps below. You can also visit the IFTTT Reddit community for additional help and troubleshooting tips from other users, or contact support for further assistance.

Refresh your connection to the service

If you’re seeing error messages in your Applet’s activity feed such “IFTTT Network Error” or your Applet is generally not triggering as expected a good first step is to refresh your connection to the service used in your Applet’s trigger. If the errors are related to your Applet’s action you can refresh the connection to the service used in your Applet’s action:

  • Head to ifttt.com or the mobile app and locate the service you’re having trouble with
  • Click ⚙︎ Settings on the top right
  • Click Reconnect
  • You’ll be taken to a page where you can reconnect the service to IFTTT
Search for service-specific tips online

Search [service name] IFTTT help online - it’s possible that the service has specific requirements for how to use IFTTT. For example, some services only offer IFTTT support with certain plans on their product.

Double-check the trigger and action fields

Verify that the intended Ingredients are being used where and how they should be. For example, don’t use a filename ingredient for an image action field.

Check your Activity Feed

This will show you are any error messages there that can help you understand what is preventing your connection from running. There is an error glossary that can help you better understand errors in your feed. If a specific service returning an error, try refreshing your connection to that service.

IoT devices (smart home products, voice assistants, etc)

If you are using a connection with an IoT device that is not running successfully, try a full reset of the device, double-check that you have connected IFTTT to the same device account where it is registered, and ensure your internet connection is strong. Also, confirm that your device is IFTTT compatible.

File Not Found error

This likely means that a required field in an Applet is empty or not filled out with the correct ingredient. Check your Applet settings to see if they can be modified. More info about this error can be found here.

Where to go for further help

If the above information doesn’t help to get your Applet working, please contact the service owner. Most services on IFTTT have tools to help them diagnose user issues as they come up. You can often locate a service’s support info by searching for [service name] IFTTT help online.

Do I need the IFTTT app installed for Applets to run?

Introduction

Not every Applet on IFTTT needs the mobile app to work. This article breaks down when the IFTTT app is required and when it isn’t—so you can better understand how your Applets run and whether having the app installed is necessary for your setup.

Services that require the IFTTT app

Some Applets rely on data or actions that come directly from your phone. For those to work, the IFTTT app must be installed and granted the necessary permissions. These are typically mobile-specific services like:

  • Android Battery
  • Android Phone Call
  • iOS Shortcuts
  • Location These Applets depend on your phone to provide key information through permissions granted to the IFTTT app, like your current location or whether your battery is low. Without the IFTTT app, these triggers or actions can’t function.

If you’re unsure if a service requires the IFTTT app to work, the best place to check is the description in the service page. If the IFTTT app is required, it’ll be noted there. For example, here is the description on the Location service page:

The IFTTT app must remain installed and signed in for these Applets to keep working. If you uninstall the app or sign out, the Applets that depend on it will stop running.

Services that do not require the IFTTT app

Many Applets work entirely through cloud-based services that are connected to your IFTTT account. These Applets do not rely on the IFTTT app and will run whether or not it’s installed on your device.

For example, an Applet like “If WyzeCam detects motion, then turn on my Hue lights” uses APIs from both services. IFTTT listens for events from WyzeCam and sends a command to Hue—all without needing anything from your mobile device.

If you’ve created Applets like these and later delete the IFTTT app, they’ll continue to run. To stop them, you’ll need to sign in to the IFTTT app or ifttt.com and turn them off.

How to tell if an Applet needs the IFTTT app to run

Applets that include services like location, phone calls, or battery level need the IFTTT app installed on your phone with the proper permissions enabled. These Applets rely on data from your mobile device to know when to run. The full list of services that require the IFTTT app to function is provided below.

On the other hand, Applets that use services connected to your IFTTT account—like smart lights, email, or calendars—run through the cloud and don’t require the app to be installed on your phone.

Services that require the IFTTT app to run

Glossary

Action

An Action is the work that IFTTT initiates when a Trigger fires. Some examples of Actions are WordPress - Create a post and Twitter - Post a tweet.

Action field

Action fields are how we add extra information to Actions. For example, the WordPress - Create a post Action has Action fields for post title, body, categories, and tags. The Twitter - Post a tweet Action includes one action field, for the text of the tweet.

Almost every Action has at least one Action field.

Applet

An Applet connects two or more services together, enabling them to do things that they wouldn’t be able to do alone.

Some example Applets include Set Android wallpaper to NASA Image of the Day or Add songs from videos you like on YouTube to a Spotify playlist.

Applets are composed of Triggers, queries, and Actions. Triggers tell an Applet to start, queries provide extra data, and Actions are the end result of an Applet run.

Applet ID

The Applet ID is an 8-character alphanumeric identifier given to each Applet on IFTTT. For example, this Applet’s ID is pc6CeRjs.

Applet run

When you create an Applet, IFTTT starts monitoring the Trigger service for new Trigger events. Every time a new Trigger event is detected, IFTTT will execute the Applet, pushing the data from the Trigger over to the Action(s). This is an Applet run.

Filter code

Filter code is a bit of JavaScript (TypeScript) code that runs right after a Trigger fires, allowing you to apply conditional logic to your Applets.

More information about building with filter code can be found here.

Ingredient

Triggers and queries contain ingredients – individual pieces of data.

Some example ingredients from the Twitter - New tweet by you trigger:

  • Text – The text of the tweet itself.
  • UserName – User name of the tweeter.
  • LinkToTweet – The URL of the tweet.
  • CreatedAt – Date and time the tweet was created.
  • TweetEmbedCode – The HTML embed code for the tweet. These ingredients can be used to fill in an Action’s Action fields.

Polling Trigger

A Trigger that fires after IFTTT performs a Trigger check and finds a new Trigger event. Polling Triggers usually run within 5 minutes for Pro and Pro+ users, and within one hour for Free users.

Query

A Query is a way to request additional data if the Trigger doesn’t provide it. Queries run immediately after Triggers, but before filter code.

Some examples of queries are Google Calendar - List Events For a Date and Facebook - List Album Photos.

Query field

Query fields are how we add extra context to queries. For example, the Query fields for the Google Calendar - List Events For a Date Query are Calendar and Date. When using the Facebook - List Album Photos Query, the only Query field is Album ID.

Realtime Trigger

A Trigger that fires after the Trigger service notifies IFTTT of a new Trigger event. Realtime Triggers tend to run within a few seconds of a Trigger event occurring.

Service

Services are the basic building blocks of IFTTT. Some example services are Facebook, Twitter, Fitbit, and Gmail. Discover our 1000+ services on the Explore page.

Trigger

A Trigger is the data that, when changed, prompts an Applet to run on IFTTT. Some example Triggers include Twitter - New tweet from search and WordPress - Any new post.

Triggers can be either realtime or polling.

Trigger check

A Trigger check is when IFTTT checks for new Trigger events. Trigger checks occur once every 5 minutes for Pro and Pro+ users, and once every hour for Free users.

Trigger event

A Trigger event is the thing that causes a given Trigger to fire. For example, a Trigger event for the Twitter - New tweet by you would be your Twitter account posting a new tweet.

Trigger field

Trigger fields are a bit like a “filter” for the data requested from a Trigger. For example, the Google Drive - New file in your folder has the Trigger field Drive folder path, which tells IFTTT which folder to monitor for new files. Or for the Spotify - New track added to a playlist Trigger, the Which playlist? dropdown tells IFTTT which playlist to watch for new tracks.

How do I report spam or abuse on IFTTT?

IFTTT has guardrails in place to keep our service running quickly, to prevent abuse of our features, and to protect people from spam and harassment. As a user, you agree not to use the IFTTT service:

  • To post or transmit, or cause to be posted or transmitted, content or communications that are libelous, defamatory, obscene, pornographic, abusive, offensive, profane, or that infringes any copyright or another right of any person;
  • To create or transmit unwanted spam to any person or any URL;
  • To create multiple accounts for the purpose of transmitting spam;
  • By any automated means for any purpose that imposes, or may impose an unreasonable or disproportionately large load on our infrastructure or interfere with the proper working of IFTTT. Users engaging in these types of behaviors may be removed from IFTTT. Learn more about our Terms of Service here.

We know spam when we see it, but things to keep an eye out for are:

  • Applets/connections that are trying to sell something in the description text;
  • Applets/connections where the title and description don’t clearly describe how they work with the services involved; and
  • Applets/connections that are promising to do something you know IFTTT can’t do. And, of course, if you see any Applets/connections that use any libelous, defamatory, obscene, pornographic, abusive, offensive, or profane language, or that infringes any copyright or another right of any person — let us know ASAP. That’s against our terms of service, and we’ll take them down.

Report spam

How does IFTTT work?

IFTTT helps connect different apps and devices. When you sign up for a free account, you can enable your apps and devices to work together to do things they couldn’t otherwise do. For example, you can back up your Instagram photos to Dropbox, have your lights turn on when you enter your home, or automatically remind a Slack channel about a meeting.

Here’s how it works:

  • Create a free account.
  • Browse the IFTTT website or app to find something that interests you.
  • Connect the services that are involved in the Applet or connection.
  • Find more Applets and connections, and repeat! Applets and connections can be built by services or users from the Developer Dashboard. You also have the power to create something custom for yourself! Go to ifttt.com/create to combine two services and make your own Applet!

How to Update the Social Media Account Linked to Your IFTTT Profile

When you create an IFTTT account, you can link it to one of your social media profiles. Once linked, your IFTTT profile picture will automatically update to match the profile picture from the connected social network. Additionally, a link to your selected social media profile will be added to your IFTTT personal page. You can view your personal page by visiting ifttt.com], replacing “[username]” with your IFTTT username.

By default, when you create an IFTTT account, it’s not linked to any of your social media account but if you want to update your social media profile, just follow these steps:

On a Web Browser

  • Go to ifttt.com/profile/edit
  • Click on the social media profile you want to link to your IFTTT account. The click ‘Set Profile’.

On the IFTTT App

  • Tap the profile icon in the bottom-right corner.
  • Click ‘edit’ on the profile circle icon.
  1. Select the social media profile you want to use.

Note: The ‘None’ option is only available in the IFTTT app, not on the website.

How to Remove Your Social Media Profile From your IFTTT Account.

On a Web Browser

Head to ifttt.com and click on the “Remove Twitter as your profile provider” link.

On the IFTTT App

  • Tap the profile icon in the bottom-right corner.
  • Click ‘edit’ on the profile circle icon.
  1. Select the social media profile and tick ‘None’.

IFTTT Service Rate Limits

Background

Some IFTTT services have limits for how often you can use them. These rate limits can be applied for a number of reasons, such as helping prevent spam or abuse. If you check your activity feed and see an error message that reads “usage limit exceeded,” that means you’ve reached one of these limits.

The majority of services on IFTTT do not have any rate limits applied by IFTTT. However some services, such as X (Twitter)and the SMS service, have limits in place.

We aim to keep these limits transparent and will send you an email when you are approaching or have reached a limit.

When Do Rate Limits Reset

  • Daily limits: Reset at midnight in your local time, based on your account’s timezone settings.
  • Monthly limits: Reset on the first day of each month at midnight local time.

What to Do if You Hit a Rate Limit

If you hit a daily or monthly limit, your Applet will start working again automatically once the limit resets.

To avoid hitting limits, consider:

  • Configuring your Applets to run less often by choosing different trigger criteria.
    • For example, if you have an Applet that uses the RSS Feed service’s New feed item trigger with the Twitter service’s Post a Tweet action and it results in more than 200 posts a day, you could switch to the New feed item matches trigger to filter by specific keywords. This will reduce how often your Applet runs.
  • Adding filters to limit when your Applet runs.
    • More information on adding filter code to an Applet is available here.

Is IFTTT available in multiple languages?

IFTTT.com is currently only available in English.

The IFTTT Android app is available in 7 different languages including English, French, German, Japanese, Portuguese, Spanish, and Chinese.

The IFTTT iOS app is available in 5 different languages including English, French, German, Japanese, and Portuguese.

We plan to make IFTTT available in even more languages in the future so that people around the world can easily use IFTTT in their native languages.

Reporting a problem on IFTTT

Many issues can be addressed by following our guide on how to contact support or give feedback, but we encourage users to file a problem report if they can’t get help from the service or notice a bug specifically with the IFTTT app or website.

Before you submit a report, please read our guidelines below.

  • We’re not able to respond to every report, but they do help us track trends and escalate issues as needed.
    • If you have a Pro or Pro+ subscription, please open a ticket following the steps in the IFTTT Pro Support section of this guide instead of using the report a problem button below
  • These reports go directly to us, not the service. We recommend reporting issues directly to the service owner if you’re having issues with a particular service.
  • Please include as much detail as possible, including any troubleshooting you’ve done already, if it is reproducible, and the Applet or connection ID if applicable. Report a problem

What is a Connection?

A Connection is the next evolution of an Applet.

Connections solve core compatibility problems for products, power personalized experiences for users, and are fully embeddable in mobile apps, websites, and emails.

Unlike Applets, which are limited to triggers, queries, and actions, Connections can power entire integrations between two services. A user connects once using a simple, one-tap button.

An example of a Connection is Park mower if weather forecast predicts frost or heavy rain by Husqvarna Automower:

To enable your first Connection, head to ifttt.com to sign up for a free IFTTT account.

What is a Query?

A Query is a way to request additional data that your Applet’s Trigger doesn’t provide. Queries run immediately after Triggers, but before filter code.

Some examples of Queries are Google Calendar - List Events For a Date and Facebook - List Album Photos.

How can I use a Query?

Queries can be used alongside Triggers to build more powerful Applets. The data from the two can be used in your Actions, or can combine with filter code to create all kinds of automations.

To add a Query to your Applet, you’ll first need to add a Trigger, after which you can click on the plus button ( + ) and select Add Query:

Queries, along with filter code, unlock Applets to go beyond if this to if this and this.

For example, the below Applet uses a Query and some filter code to notify you when the ISS passes overhead, but only if it’s nighttime:

  • Trigger: Space - ISS passes over a specific location
  • Query: Weather Underground - Current weather
  • filter code: Skip the Action if the current time is after sunrise and before sunset (when the sun is up)
  • Action: Notifications - Send a notification from the IFTTT app

Do I need to use filter code to use a Query?

filter code is not required to use Queries.

For example, the following Applet uses a Google Calendar Query to send an email every day containing all calendar events for the upcoming day:

  • Trigger: Date & Time - Every day at
  • Query: Google Calendar - List Events For a Date
  • Action: Email - Send me an email

What is an Applet?

An Applet connects two or more apps or devices together. It’s an automation or integration/connection between two services that enables you to do something that those services couldn’t do on their own.

  • Applets are composed of Triggers and Actions
    • Triggers tell an Applet to start, and actions are the end result of an Applet run To use an Applet, you’ll need to create a free IFTTT account. Then you’ll need to connect your apps and devices to IFTTT, so we can help them talk to each other.

Applet facts:

  • When IFTTT launched in 2010, Applets were called Recipes
    • Our name “IFTTT” comes from “if this, then that,” because the first Recipes had one Trigger and one Action
  • Today, Applets can have more than one action (“if this, then that, and that”) and/or conditional filters (“if this, and that, then that”)
  • Over one billion Applets run every single month As an IFTTT user, you can turn on Applets published by our partners or other users, or create your own.

What is IFTTT?

At IFTTT, we believe that everything works better together!

Our app allows you to do more with over 1000 different apps and services, including Twitter, Dropbox, Evernote, Fitbit, and Google Assistant.

  • On IFTTT, we call these services. See the full list of all IFTTT-enabled services here. We connect services together into Applets - automations that allow you to do things your apps and devices can’t do on their own.

  • For example, you could use IFTTT to sync iOS Calendar events to Google Calendar

What does IFTTT stand for?

IFTTT is short for “If This Then That”.

(To pronounce IFTTT, just say “Gift” without the G!)

What does IFTTT mean?

One of the principal ideas in coding is If This, Then That. Software developers define a condition, and then tell the program what to do if that condition is met. IFTTT’s no-code interface allows anybody to set a condition (if this) and combine it with a result (then that) by using Applets.

Before we shortened our name to IFTTT, we were called ‘If This, Then That’ because Applets would have one trigger and one action: If this happens — then that happens.

  • For example, “If you enter your home, then turn on your Hue lights.” These days, we just call ourselves “IFTTT” because there’s so much more you can do:

  • Applets can have multiple actions (If this, then that, that, and that)

  • Applets can run when you tell them to, at the touch of a Button (That!)

  • And some Applets even have filters that mean they only run when a specific condition is met (If this and this, then that) You can turn on Applets that already exist on our explore page, or you can make something custom for yourself.

What is the difference between polling Applets and realtime Applets?

On IFTTT, there are two types of Applets, polling Applets and realtime Applets. Polling Applets use polling triggers, while realtime Applets use realtime triggers.

When you’re on a service page, scroll down to see the available Triggers, Queries, and Actions for that service. Triggers will be labeled as either Realtime or Polling, so you can easily identify how they work.

Example of Realtime Triggers on the Camera Widget service page

Polling Applets

Polling Applets run after IFTTT reaches out to the trigger service and finds a new trigger event. These trigger checks occur every 5 minutes for Pro and Pro+ users, and every hour for Free users.

When viewing the Applet’s activity feed, the heading Successful trigger checks indicates the Applet is using a polling trigger:

Because of the reactive nature of polling triggers, we expect polling Applets to run within 5 minutes of a trigger event occurring for Pro and Pro+ users, and within one hour for Free users.

Realtime Applets

Realtime Applets run after the trigger service notifies IFTTT of a new trigger event. Since IFTTT is notified proactively of new trigger events, realtime Applets normally run within a few seconds of a trigger event occurring.

When viewing the Applet’s activity feed, the heading Listening for trigger notifications indicates the Applet is using a real time trigger:

What determines if a trigger is Polling or Real Time?

Whether a trigger is polling or real-time depends on how the service creator set up their IFTTT integration. While service owners can update triggers to use the IFTTT real-time API, you won’t be able to change this setting yourself when configuring your Applet.

When do I contact support?

IFTTT is a platform that serves as an intermediary between hundreds of different services. How you get support largely depends on what type of problem you’re having. Here are our guidelines on how to get the support you need.

IFTTT Pro Support

If you have a Pro or Pro+ subscription you can contact our technical support through IFTTT.com or the IFTTT app. See below for instructions on how to contact support:

  • Web: Sign in to your IFTTT Pro or Pro+ account and reach out directly via the Help button on any ifttt.com page
  • iOS: Sign in to the IFTTT app using your IFTTT Pro or Pro+ account, tap profile, Help center, then tap the message icon in the top right to open a ticket.
  • Android: Sign in to the IFTTT app using your IFTTT Pro or Pro+ account, tap profile, Help center, then tap the help icon in the bottom right to open a ticket.

Billing Help

If you’re having trouble starting a subscription, questions about the different subscription options, or need help with an existing subscription you can contact our billing team by emailing Billing@ifttt.com.

Problems with an Applet or service

In most cases, following our guides on troubleshooting can help with common issues. If you still need help after that, follow the guide below.

  • Do a search online for the problem, perhaps you’re not alone! There may be tips others have shared, or solutions to problems people have posted.
    • 💡Protip: Searching for Service IFTTT help is a good place to start.
  • Check out the IFTTT Reddit community to search for your issue or ask other users for help and troubleshooting tips.
    • 💡Protip: These are your best places for support with the more DIY features of IFTTT, such as Webhooks.
  • Get in touch with the service’s support. Most services on IFTTT have tools available to them to help diagnose problems, and many have guides on how to best use IFTTT in their documentation.
  • Report a problem on IFTTT. We don’t respond to all problem reports but we do track them for trends and will escalate as needed.

Problems with your IFTTT account

Login issues, two-step verification, account access – all issues we’d be happy to help with. Check out our guides and send us a message if you still need help.

  • 💡IFTTT Pro support: Sign in to your IFTTT Pro account and reach out directly via the Help button on any ifttt.com page Help with account access

Need to locate an IFTTT account

Is there something running via IFTTT that you can’t locate the account for? We can help you find it if you’re not having any luck.

  • 💡IFTTT Pro support: Sign in to your IFTTT Pro account and reach out directly via the Help button on any ifttt.com page Help with locating an account

Spam and abuse on IFTTT

If you see spam on IFTTT or someone is using IFTTT for any violations of our terms of service, we definitely want to know about it!

  • 💡IFTTT Pro support: Sign in to your IFTTT Pro account and reach out directly via the Help button on any ifttt.com page Report spam or abuse

Billing, plans, and pricing

Billing FAQ

Which payment methods can I use?

We accept all major credit, debit cards, and direct debit when you subscribe on the IFTTT website. Direct debits are currently available only to customers in the United States. To subscribe via PayPal, check out these instructions.

You can also subscribe via Apple App Store or Google Play Store in-app payment from the IFTTT mobile app ( iOS / Android ).

Can I get a free trial?

You sure can! When you upgrade your Free Tier account to IFTTT Pro or Pro+, we grant you a 7-day free trial. If you don’t like it and cancel before the trial runs out, you won’t be charged.

Please note, if your account previously had a free trial you won’t be eligible for another trial.

How is my subscription renewed?

We automatically renew your subscription on the same day each month or year.

How can I cancel my subscription?

If you started your subscription on the IFTTT website, cancel your subscription by heading to the Billing page and clicking Cancel.

If you signed up via in-app payment, follow these instructions for canceling your subscription ( Apple App Store / Google Play Store ).

Please note that this simply cancels the automatic renewal of your subscription. You will retain access to Pro/Pro+ features until the end of your current billing cycle, and will not be charged again by IFTTT.

Where can I update my billing information?

Head to the Billing page and click Edit payment info.

What happens if a payment fails?

If we are not able to charge your account upon renewal, we will let you know via email and try again later. We will keep your subscription active for 14 days so you can fix any issues or update the payment method on your Billing page.

After 14 days, your account will be automatically downgraded to the Free plan.

Why is my payment failing?

Double-check that the information you are entering is correct, including the expiration date and security code. Some banks and card issuers limit online transactions by default. You can reach out to your bank and ask if that’s the case or if there are any other reasons why the transaction was not successful.

Can I get a receipt?

Sure! You can download receipts from the Billing page. Let us know if you are missing any.

Do you offer refunds?

No, all payments are final and non-refundable.

Why is my account showing as Pro in the app but Free on IFTTT.com?

IFTTT subscriptions are managed at the account level, not based on the device or platform you are using.

If you see an active Pro or Pro+ subscription in the mobile app but a Free tier badge on IFTTT.com, this usually means you are signed into two different accounts.

To resolve this, open the mobile app while signed into the account showing your Pro or Pro+ subscription. Go to the Profile tab, tap account and check the email address listed under your account details. Then make sure you are signing into IFTTT.com using the same email address.

In some cases, using a social login such as Google or Apple (learn more about social login here) can unintentionally create a second account, which leads to this mismatch.

Can I use IFTTT for free?

Yes! You can create an IFTTT account for free, with no subscription required. The free plan lets you enable up to two Applets, each with one trigger and one action. Pro and Pro+ plans offer additional features, and you can see a full comparison at ifttt.com/plans.

Can I change from Pro+ to Pro?

If you subscribed through our website, you can downgrade to Pro from the Billing page in your web browser: ifttt.com/billing. Click Switch plans and choose the Pro plan.

Your downgrade will take effect immediately. Any unused time remaining on your Pro+ subscription will be applied as a credit toward future charges.

How do I downgrade to the free plan?

When your account is canceled, it will automatically be downgraded to the free plan, and no further action is needed on your part.

What currency is shown on ifttt.com/plans?

Pricing on ifttt.com/plans is displayed in USD (U.S. dollars).

If you would like to pay in your local currency, please contact billing@ifttt.com for assistance.

Subscriptions purchased through the Google Play Store or Apple App Store are automatically displayed and billed in your local currency, based on your store settings.

Can I subscribe annually/yearly to IFTTT Pro and Pro+?

Yes! To subscribe annually to IFTTT Pro or Pro+ as a new user, please follow these steps:

  • Sign into your account. If you are new to IFTTT, create an account
  • Go to ifttt.com/plans
  • Select the appropriate plan, Pro or Pro+
  • Select the payment option Billed yearly
  • Complete your payment details
  • Clicked Complete purchase If your subscription was created via AppStore or Google Play, you could update it to be billed yearly through the subscription management interface of the respective store.

To alter an existing subscription started with a credit card and switch to annual billing, please contact the support team and request that your subscription be updated at the end of your current billing cycle.

Can I subscribe to IFTTT Pro or Pro+ with PayPal?

Yes! PayPal is available in these countries, and can be linked to Apple Payments or Google Pay.

Here’s how to set up PayPal:

  • Select Your name

IFTTT Plans at a glance

For users, IFTTT comes in three flavors - Free, Pro and Pro+. Visit the Plans page for pricing and a full comparison of all IFTTT plans.

Free

On the Free plan, you can enable a limited number of Applets. Either create them yourself from the Applet composer or choose from the millions of published Applets that have already been made from the ifttt.com/explore.

Applets on the free tier can include one trigger and one action. You can also add a delay before the action in your Applets.

The Free plan has no subscription fees.

Pro

The Pro plan has extra perks.

  • You can create and enable up to 20 Applets
  • Multiple actions can be included in a single Applet
  • Pro users’ Applets run faster
  • Increased rate limits for certain services
  • Applets can include triggers and actions from Pro services such as Twitter and Webhooks

Pro+

Pro+ unlocks our best and most powerful tools.

  • Create and enable unlimited Applets
  • Add logic to your Applets with queries and filter code
  • Connect multiple accounts per service
  • Access to the IFTTT AI Services

Do you offer a free trial?

When you sign up for Pro or Pro+, you’ll receive a 7-day free trial automatically, as long as you haven’t already had a trial.

Do you support annual billing?

Yes! You can subscribe to any paid plan on a monthly or yearly basis.

Why is my card being declined?

Top reasons for card declines include incorrect CVC (the 3 digit code on the back of your card or the 4 digit code on the front of your American Express card), expired card, or insufficient funds. You can check to see if you have entered the CVC correctly or whether your card is expired by viewing the payment method details displayed on ifttt.com/billing .

If you’re still having trouble, we’d recommend trying a different card. You can update your default payment method by going to ifttt.com/billing and pressing the Edit payment info.

Declines may also be caused by the fraud protection that is applied automatically by your bank or financial institution.

Your bank or card issuer can provide a more detailed explanation about why your card may have been declined. Reach out to them directly to see what they suggest.

Connecting and managing services

Can I connect more than one account to a service?

Yes! Our Pro+ plan supports multiple connected accounts per service. Your IFTTT account can support Applets for work and personal accounts, or when you have more than one account per service.

Check out our guide to Multiple account connections to get started.

Connecting to a service on IFTTT

You can connect to a service on IFTTT in many ways:

Through the explore page:

  • Visit the explore page and enter the service name in the search bar
  • Click or tap on the card for the service you searched for
  • Click or tap Connect on the service page
  • You will be redirected to the service for authorization
  • Enter your username and password, or use credentials from an OAuth service if supported (e.g. Sign in with Google)
  • Once authorization has been complete, you will be returned to IFTTT

Through the services page:

  • Visit the services page and search for your service
  • Click or tap Connect on the service page
  • You will be redirected to the service for authorization
  • Enter your username and password, or use credentials from an OAuth service if supported (e.g. Sign in with Google)
  • Once authorization has been complete, you will be returned to IFTTT

By connecting to a premade Applet if the service is not already connected:

  • Visit the services page and enter the service name in the search bar
  • Select a premade Applet that connects to the service
  • Click or tap Connect on the Applet page
  • You will be redirected to the service for authorization
  • Enter your username and password, or use credentials from an OAuth service if supported (e.g. Sign in with Google)
  • Once authorization has been complete, you will be returned to IFTTT

By creating an Applet if the service is not already connected:

  • Choose a trigger, action, or query from the service while creating an Applet in the Applet composer
  • You will be redirected to the service for authorization
  • Enter your username and password, or use credentials from an OAuth service if supported (e.g. Sign in with Google)
  • Once authorization has been complete, you will be returned to IFTTT

  • Visit the Zoom service page
  • Click or tap Connect on the service page
  • You will be redirected to Zoom for authorization
  • Enter your username and password, or use credentials from an OAuth service if supported (e.g. Sign in with Google)
  • Once authorization has been complete, you will be returned to IFTTT

By connecting to a premade Applet if the service is not already connected:

  • Visit the Zoom service page
  • Select a premade Applet that connects to Zoom
  • Click or tap Connect on the Applet page
  • You will be redirected to Zoom for authorization
  • Enter your username and password, or use credentials from an OAuth service if supported (e.g. Sign in with Google)
  • Once authorization has been complete, you will be returned to IFTTT

By creating an Applet if the service is not already connected:

  • Choose a trigger, query, or action from Zoom while creating an Applet in the Applet composer
  • You will be redirected to Zoom for authorization
  • Enter your username and password, or use credentials from an OAuth service if supported (e.g. Sign in with Google)
  • Once authorization has been complete, you will be returned to IFTTT

Disconnecting a service

To disconnect a service from your IFTTT account, you first need to visit the individual service page. This can be found through your services, the explore page, or the services page when logged in to IFTTT.

When on the service page, select Settings, and click Remove.

Note that this will remove all Applets and connections for that service from your IFTTT account. If you choose to reconnect the service, you’ll be able to restore any removed Applets from your IFTTT archive.

Removing IFTTT from your Zoom app

  • Login to your Zoom account and navigate to the Zoom App Marketplace.
  • Click Manage > Installed Apps, or search for the IFTTT app.
  • Click the IFTTT app.
  • Click Uninstall

How do I change the account associated with a service?

Background

Applets use the Triggers, Queries, and Actions associated with the account you’ve connected to each service on IFTTT. For example, if you’ve connected a specific Facebook Page to the Facebook Pages service, any Triggers will run based on updates to that Page, and any Actions will post to it. If you’d like to change which account is connected to a service, follow these steps:

Updating the account connected to a service

  • Go to the service page you’d like to change
  • Select ⚙︎ Settings
  • Select Reconnect
  • Enter the login credentials for the account you want to use and follow the on screen prompts By default, any Applets that were using the previously connected account will automatically switch to use the new one.

You won’t need to make any changes as long as the inputs are still valid. If something no longer matches, you’ll need to edit the Applet to update those details. For example, if you switch which Trello account is connected and the new one doesn’t include the board that an Applet uses, that Applet won’t be able to run until it’s updated.

To fix this, open the Applet’s settings and update the inputs to match your new account.

Troubleshooting

If you’re trying to change the account connected to a service that you’re currently logged into in your browser, make sure to log out of that account on the service’s website before following the steps above.

When you connect a service on IFTTT, it will automatically use the account that’s already signed in on that service’s website. If you stay logged in, IFTTT will default to using that account again, even if you meant to connect a different one.

For example, if you’re updating the X (formerly Twitter) service to use a different account, go to x.com, log out of your current account, then visit ifttt.com/twitter and follow the steps in the section above to connect the correct one.

Connecting multiple accounts to a service

On the Free and Pro tiers you can connect a single account to each service on IFTTT. If you’d like to connect multiple accounts from one service to a single IFTTT account that is available on the Pro+ tier, You can view more details about connecting multiple accounts to a service on our help center here.

How do I change the location for Weather Applets?

The Weather Underground service allows the location to be set for each Applet. This means that you can have multiple Weather Applets, each with a different location.

Instead of visiting the settings page of the Weather service to set your location, you’ll set it when you turn on or edit an Applet:

How does Google Assistant Voice Match work with IFTTT Applets?

Newer Google Assistant devices have a built-in Voice Match feature that lets individuals train Google Assistant to recognize their voices for a more personalized experience.

This has several implications for how Google Assistant Applets work on these devices:

  • Voice Matched users are able to trigger their own Applets
  • Voice Matched users are not able to trigger Applets from other IFTTT accounts
  • Non-Voice Matched users are able to trigger all Applets User’s Applet

  • For devices that do not support Voice Match/have Voice Match disabled, all users are able to trigger all Applets

How to connect Homekit with IFTTT

Apple’s HomeKit is a smart home platform designed for seamless integration with Apple devices, offering secure and intuitive control over smart accessories like lights, locks, and thermostats. With built-in privacy protections and local processing, HomeKit ensures a reliable and safe smart home experience. Integrating HomeKit with IFTTT can further expand its automation capabilities, enabling connections with third-party services and smart devices that may not have native HomeKit support.

What is HomeKit?

HomeKit provides a unified platform for managing and automating smart home devices through the Apple Home app, Siri, and Shortcuts. Users can create custom scenes, triggers, and automations to streamline daily routines, whether controlling devices remotely, scheduling actions, or using voice commands. By leveraging the Shortcuts app, HomeKit users can build advanced automations, linking smart devices with apps and services beyond Apple’s ecosystem for even greater flexibility.

What is IFTTT?

Trusted by millions worldwide, IFTTT (If This, Then That) is a user-friendly automation platform that connects apps, devices, and services without requiring any coding. It works through Applets—customizable automations that link Triggers (“if this”) to Actions (“then that”), enabling seamless automation across different platforms. With a vast range of supported services, IFTTT allows users to create personalized automations tailored to their needs.

Get started with IFTTT today and unlock a smarter, more connected digital experience with one of the most versatile automation tools available.

How to connect IFTTT with Apple HomeKit

You can connect IFTTT with HomeKit using the Shortcuts app as a bridge. The Home app allows you to connect accessories like cameras, smart lights, garage doors, and more. See Apple’s full list of supported accessories here. You’ll create an automation in the Home app that triggers a Shortcut, which in turn triggers an IFTTT Applet using the iOS Shortcuts Service.

Step 1: Set up a Home Hub

Apple HomeKit requires a HomePod, HomePod Mini, or Apple TV to add accessories and enable automations.

Follow Apple’s guide on setting up a Home Hub if you haven’t done this yet.

Step 2: Add your accessories

You can only add accessories to the Home app using an iPhone or iPad.

  • Turn on the accessory and ensure it’s near your device.
  • Tap the + button at the top of the Home app and select Add Accessory.
  • If it doesn’t appear automatically, follow the instructions to add it manually. Tip: You can organize accessories into Rooms and group Rooms into Zones for easier control.
Step 3: Create a Scene

In the Home app:

  • Tap the + button and choose Add Scene.
  • Select Custom, then give your scene a name (e.g., “IFTTT 1” or “IFTTT 2”).
  • Add at least one accessory to save the scene—choose something that works in tandem with your IFTTT Applet (e.g., a light turns off in HomeKit while a smart plug turns on via IFTTT).
  • Tap Done to finish creating the scene.
Step 4: Set up your automation

In the Home app:

  • Tap the + button and select Add Automation.
  • Choose how you want to trigger the automation:
    • An Accessory is Controlled — triggered when an accessory turns on/off.
    • A Sensor Detects Something — for sensor-based triggers.
  • Select the accessory that will initiate the automation and tap Next.
  • Choose the scene you created earlier (e.g., “IFTTT 1”) and tap Next.
  • Tap Done to save the automation.
Step 5: Create an Applet in IFTTT

Before creating your Shortcut, set up an IFTTT Applet:

  • Head to ifttt.com/create to begin creating an Applet.
  • Select the iOS Shortcuts service in IFTTT.
  • Choose the Shortcut Automation Started trigger.
  • Combine this with your desired action (e.g., “Turn on Smart Plug”).
  • Save the Applet and customize its name for easy identification in Shortcuts.
Step 6: Create a Shortcut.

In the Shortcuts app:

  • Tap the + button to create a new Shortcut.
  • Scroll down and select Trigger Applet under the IFTTT app.
  • Tap Select an Applet, then find and select the Applet you created in Step 5.
  • Tap Done to save your Shortcut.
Step 7: Create a Shortcuts Automation

In the Shortcuts app:

  • Go to the Automation tab and tap the +.
  • Choose Scene is Activated as the trigger.
  • Select the scene you created in Step 3.
  • Tap Next and add the Shortcut you created in Step 6 as the action.
  • Tap Done to save your automation.
And You’re Done!

Now, whenever the scene is activated in the Home app, the Shortcut will run and trigger your IFTTT Applet. This allows you to bridge HomeKit and IFTTT seamlessly!

How to connect HubSpot to IFTTT

What is IFTTT?

Trusted by millions worldwide, IFTTT (If This, Then That) is a user-friendly automation platform that connects apps, devices, and services without requiring any coding. It works through Applets—customizable automations that link Triggers (“if this”) to Actions (“then that”), enabling seamless automation across different platforms. With a vast range of supported services, IFTTT allows users to create personalized automations tailored to their needs.

How to connect HubSpot to IFTTT

To start creating HubSpot automations using IFTTT, you will first need to connect your HubSpot account.

To connect IFTTT from your HubSpot account, follow the instructions in this HubSpot Knowledge Base article.

To connect HubSpot from your IFTTT account, follow the instructions below.

  • Visit ifttt.com
  • Click Connect HubSpot
  • Sign into your HubSpot account
  • Select your HubSpot account and click Connect Account
  • Review the requested permissions on this screen and then click Connect app
  • You will be redirected to the HubSpot service page on IFTTT. A banner confirming the service is connected will briefly appear on screen once the connection is complete.

How to configure and use HubSpot in your IFTTT Applets

Once connected, you can start creating or enabling Applets that use the HubSpot service’s Triggers, Queries, and Actions.

To learn how to create Applets, please see our guide How do I create Applets?

For example, to automatically save new contacts to Google Sheets:

  • Navigate to ifttt.com/create
  • Click Add to the right of “If This”
  • Search for and select the HubSpot service
  • Select the card titled New Contact Created then press Create Trigger
  • Next, click Add to the right of “Then That”
  • Search for and select the Google Sheets service
  • Select the card titled Add Row to Spreadsheet
  • Configure the fields as per your preference then press Create Action
  • Follow the remaining prompts to save your Applet Once enabled, any time a new contact is added to HubSpot it will also be added to your Google Sheet.

You can follow these steps to create any Applet with HubSpot that you’d like.

How to disconnect the IFTTT HubSpot service

If you’d like to remove your HubSpot account’s connection to IFTTT, you can follow the steps below:

  • Navigate to ifttt.com
  • Click Settings in the top right
  • Click Remove service next to your connection to disconnect the service Please note, disconnecting the HubSpot service from your IFTTT account will remove any Applets that use the HubSpot service.

Any HubSpot data within IFTTT will be removed according to our terms and conditions at ifttt.com.

How to uninstall the IFTTT app from HubSpot

To uninstall IFTTT from your HubSpot account, follow the instructions in this HubSpot Knowledge Base article.

How to connect IFTTT to Alexa

Owned by Amazon, Alexa is a leading smart home platform. Its ecosystem includes smart speakers, displays, and integrations with a wide range of smart home devices. Alexa works with IFTTT using 3rd party skills, enabling its devices to be part of thousands of custom automations.

What is Alexa?

Amazon Alexa is a voice-activated assistant that works with smart devices to automate tasks like adjusting lights, thermostats, or appliances. It allows users to set up custom triggers and actions for easier control of their home environment. These integrations help streamline everyday routines and make home automation more accessible.

Utilizing Alexa in combination with IFTTT expands your automation capabilities, enabling integration with smart devices that do not natively support IFTTT.

What is IFTTT?

Used by over 30 million people worldwide, IFTTT (If This, Then That) is a no-code platform designed for building powerful automations. At the core of IFTTT are Applets, which link Triggers (“if this”) to Actions (“then that”) to create seamless workflows. Each service on IFTTT offers a variety of Triggers and Actions, allowing for millions of potential combinations.

Join IFTTT today to simplify your life with one of the most trusted automation tools available.

Does Alexa still work with IFTTT?

Although Alexa’s native IFTTT integration was discontinued on October 31, 2023, there are still many ways to connect IFTTT Applets with Alexa routines. Popular services on IFTTT that facilitate Alexa integration include Alexa Voice Monkey, Notify My Echo, Virtual Buttons and Alexa Doorbell.

How to trigger Alexa Routines with IFTTT

To trigger Alexa Routines from IFTTT, you can use the Alexa Voice Monkey service.

Voice Monkey makes it simple to trigger Alexa Routines through IFTTT. By setting up a virtual device in Voice Monkey and linking it to an IFTTT Applet, you can automate your Alexa routines with ease.

Here’s how:

Step 1: Set up Voice Monkey

Start by creating a Voice Monkey account if you haven’t already. Follow the onboarding guide to get everything set up.

Step 2: Create a Virtual Trigger Device

Once your account is ready, navigate to the Devices section in the Voice Monkey console. Here, create a Routine Trigger Device—this acts as a virtual doorbell that can be used as a trigger in an Alexa Routine.

Give it a recognizable name like “Bedroom light Trigger” or anything that suits your automation needs.

Step 3: Set up your Alexa routine

Now, configure an Alexa Routine that responds to the virtual device:

  • Open the Alexa app, tap More, then select Routines.
  • Tap the ‘+’ icon to create a new routine and give it a name (e.g., “Turn on Bedroom Lights”).
  • Under ‘When This Happens’, choose Smart Home, then select the virtual device you just created.
  • In ‘Add Action’, specify what the routine should do—like turning on smart bulbs in the kitchen.
  • Save the routine.
Step 4: Create an IFTTT Applet to Trigger Alexa

Now, set up an IFTTT Applet that will activate your Voice Monkey device:

  • Go to IFTTT and create a new Applet.
  • In the ‘If This’ section, select your trigger.
  • In the ‘Then That’ section, choose Alexa Voice Monkey and select Trigger Monkey (routine).
  • If you haven’t already connected to the Voice Monkey service, you’ll be prompted to sign in with Amazon to link your Voice Monkey account.
  • Once linked, select the virtual device you created earlier (eg. “Bedroom Light Trigger”.
  • Save and activate the Applet. Now, whenever the trigger event occurs, it will activate the virtual device, which in turn will run your Alexa Routine—whether that’s turning on a light, playing music, or anything else you’ve set up.

That’s it! You’re all set to automate Alexa Routines using IFTTT and Voice Monkey. 🚀

How to Trigger IFTTT Applets with Alexa

Using Alexa to trigger an IFTTT Applet is simple using Voice Money. You can refer to their guide for How to make an HTTP request from Alexa.

In IFTTT, use the Webhooks service to receive the Web request, which will can trigger any Applet.

How to connect IFTTT to Google Home

Google Assistant makes it easy to control your smart home, get things done, and find answers—all just by using your voice. When you combine it with IFTTT, you unlock even more possibilities by connecting it to over 1000 other services. Want to turn on the lights, post updates to social media, or control other smart devices with a simple voice command? With IFTTT and Google Assistant, it’s all possible. This guide will outline how to get started using Google Home and IFTTT.

If you’re ready to connect Google Home on IFTTT click this link to get started!

What is Google Home?

Google Home is a smart home platform that helps you control and automate your connected devices. You can manage everything from your phone, tablet, or smart display, or simply use your voice by saying, “Hey Google.” With Google Home, you can control your lights, cameras, and thermostats, set up custom routines to automate tasks, and monitor your home with helpful alerts.

How to connect Google Home to IFTTT?

To get started creating Applets using Google Home and IFTTT, you’ll first need to connect to the IFTTT Google Assistant service. To do so, you can follow these steps:

  • Download the Google Home app on iOS or Android
  • Tap settings your profile icon in the top right, then tap Home Settings
  • Next, select the Works with Google option
  • Type IFTTT into the search bar, then tap the IFTTT listing
  • Follow the on screen prompts to sign into your IFTTT account, then press Authorize

What can you automate with Google Home and IFTTT?

The Google Assistant service on IFTTT supports the Activate scene trigger that lets you use a custom voice command to run an Applet. You can set your preferred command in the Scene name field when configuring the Applet’s trigger. Once the Applet is enabled, simply say, “Hey Google, activate [Scene name]” to run your Applet.

This trigger can be paired with any action on IFTTT. Some popular examples include using your Google Assistant to close your MyQ Garage door or using a voice command to VOIP call your phone to find it if you’ve misplaced it.

If you’d prefer not to include the word “activate” in the command to your Google Assistant that is possible to do using Google Routines. Click here for a guide on How to Use Routines in Google Assistant V2 Without Saying ‘Activate’.

IFTTT is the most trusted automation system on the market. Over 30 million users have used IFTTT since 2010 to connect all of their favorite tools and apps. The basic idea behind IFTTT is to create Applets that trigger when certain conditions are met. For example, use your voice to turn on the Hue lights. These Applets are created by IFTTT engineers and other users like you and can be enabled in just a couple of clicks. How to Connect IFTTT to Google Nest

Google Nest Thermostat helps you maintain a comfortable and energy-efficient home by automatically adjusting your heating and cooling. By connecting it to IFTTT, you can unlock even more automation possibilities. This guide will walk you through how to connect your Nest Thermostat to IFTTT and what you can automate with it.

What is Google Nest?

Google Nest Thermostat is a smart thermostat that allows you to control the temperature of your home remotely using the Google Home app or voice commands. It can learn your schedule, adjust temperatures accordingly, and help reduce energy consumption. With IFTTT, you can take automation even further by linking Nest to other smart home devices and services.

How to Connect Google Nest to IFTTT

To get started automating your Google Nest Thermostat with IFTTT, you’ll first need to connect to the Google Nest Thermostat service. You can do so by following these steps:

  • Head to the Google Nest Thermostat service page on IFTTT
  • Press the “sign in with Google” button
  • If prompted, sign into your Google account then press Next
  • You’ll be directed to a screen with the heading “Google Nest permissions”
  • Enable the slider under step 1 titled Allow IFTTT for Nest Thermostat to see information about your home
  • Next, enable the slider or sliders under step 2 Select devices to connect with IFTTT for Nest Thermostat, then press Next and follow the remaining prompts.
  • If you have multiple thermostats you can select one or both to use with IFTTT Once connected, start creating or enabling pre-made Applets to automate your thermostat.
What Can You Automate with Google Nest and IFTTT?

With IFTTT, you can automate your Google Nest Thermostat based on temperature and humidity changes. The Google Nest thermostat service supports triggers, meaning it can be used in the If This portion of an Applet then paired with any action on IFTTT. The service supports the following four triggers:

  • Temperature rises above: Trigger an action when the temperature surpasses a specified threshold. Example: Turn on a smart fan when the temperature exceeds 75°F.
  • Temperature drops below: Trigger an action when the temperature falls below a set level. Example: If the indoor temperature drops below 65 degrees, use the Weather Underground Current Weather query to check the outdoor temperature. If it’s warmer outside than inside, receive a notification suggesting you open a window instead of turning on the heater.
  • Humidity rises above: Activate an action when humidity surpasses a set level. Example: Turn on a dehumidifier if humidity exceeds 70%.
  • Humidity drops below: Trigger an action when humidity falls below a given threshold. Example: Activate a humidifier when humidity drops below 30%.

How to connect IFTTT to LinkedIn

Background

LinkedIn is a professional networking platform that helps you share updates, promote content, and stay connected with colleagues and communities. Many people use LinkedIn to share announcements, articles, and links related to their work or business.

When you connect LinkedIn to IFTTT you can automate posting to your LinkedIn profile using activity from other apps and services. This makes it easy to keep your profile up to date without posting manually each time.

How to connect LinkedIn to IFTTT

To start creating Linkedin automations using IFTTT, you will first need to connect your LinkedIn account.

  • Sign into your IFTTT account
  • Visit ifttt.com/linkedin
  • Click Connect
  • Follow the on-screen prompts to authorize IFTTT Once connected, you can start creating or enabling Applets that use the LinkedIn service’s Actions.

What can you automate with LinkedIn and IFTTT?

The LinkedIn service on IFTTT supports several actions that let you share content directly to your LinkedIn profile:

  • Share an update: This action will share a text update on your LinkedIn profile.

  • Share a link: This action will share a link on your LinkedIn profile.

  • Share an update with image: This action will share an update with an image on your LinkedIn profile.

  • Share an update with video: This action will share an update with a video on your LinkedIn profile. These actions can be paired with many different triggers on IFTTT. Popular examples include:

  • Sharing updates from an RSS feed to LinkedIn automatically

  • Sharing new Instagram photos to LinkedIn when you post on Instagram

  • Posting new WordPress blog updates to LinkedIn as link posts

Does the LinkedIn service support Triggers?

At this time, the LinkedIn service only supports Actions, meaning it can be used as the Then That portion of an Applet. It does not support Triggers, so it cannot be used as the If This portion of an Applet.

Can I post to a LinkedIn Company Page?

At this time, the LinkedIn service on IFTTT works only with personal LinkedIn profiles. Company Pages cannot be connected or used in an Applet.

Owned by Chamberlain, MyQ is a next generation smart home provider. Their products include smart locks, smart cameras, and smart garages. The most popular MyQ Applets are paired with Services like the location service, mobile OS Services like iOS Shortcuts or Android Device, and other smart home providers like Philips Hue, TP-Link Kasa and August.

What is MyQ?

MyQ is a smart home platform that lets you control and monitor your garage door opener, gate, and other compatible devices from anywhere using your smartphone. With the myQ app, you can open and close your garage, check the door’s status, and receive real-time notifications for added peace of mind. It also works with popular smart home services to enhance your home’s automation. When paired with IFTTT, MyQ lets you create custom automations that keep your home secure and your routines running smoothly.

What is IFTTT?

IFTTT (If This, Then That) is a no-code automation platform, trusted by over 30 million users worldwide. The main function of IFTTT is to allow users to create Applets. These combine a Trigger (if this), with an Action (then that). Every Service includes at least one trigger or action, but many Services have over 10 different triggers, and actions, allowing for endless possible Applets. Get started with IFTTT today and join the most trusted automation platform on the market!

How to connect MyQ to IFTTT

Once you’ve created an IFTTT account, connecting to MyQ is easy:

  • Open the MyQ Service page.
  • Click connect.
  • Enter your login credentials. Passwords are not stored anywhere on IFTTT, and your data is not sold to any 3rd party.
  • You will be redirected to the MyQ Service page, where you can now create an Applet. To manage your connection to MyQ, you can head to your MyQ Settings page and either reconnect, or remove your account.

What can you automate with MyQ and IFTTT?

The MyQ service supports four triggers and three actions that can be used in Applets to create automations.

The MyQ triggers enable you to run an Applet when your garage door opens or closes, or when your light turns on or off. Popular examples include turning on other smart home devices when the door moves or having an announcement play when it opens or closes:

The MyQ actions let you turn your light on or off and close your garage door. Popular examples include automatically closing your garage door when you leave home or when you lock your smart lock:

Can you open MyQ Garage door with IFTTT?

Currently, the MyQ service on IFTTT does not support an open door action. If a service is missing a trigger, query, or action that you’d find helpful, you can request new features by clicking the Suggest new button on the Details tab of the service page. Suggestions submitted here go directly to the team managing that service. You can explore the triggers and actions available for MyQ by visiting the MyQ service details page.

What can you automate with iOS and IFTTT?

Connect iOS Reminders to IFTTT

iOS Reminders is an application for iOS and OS X that allows you to set notifications for yourself and create to-do lists. Quickly sync and create reminders from voice assistants, email, task managers, and more.

To get started using iOS photos with IFTTT, go to the iOS Reminders service page on IFTTT and tap connect. Once connected ensure you’re signed into the IFTTT app on your iPhone and grant the app the reminders permission. The Reminders permission can be granted by going to Settings > Apps > IFTTT, then tapping the slider to grant the Reminders permission.

If a reminder is created using the desktop Reminders app or through iCloud in a desktop web browser, it will still sync to the Reminders app on iPhone, as long as you are signed into the same iCloud account on both devices.

When that synced reminder appears in the iPhone Reminders app, the Applet will trigger, provided the IFTTT app is installed on the iPhone.

In other words, Applets can also fire for reminders created on a desktop device, as long as:

  • The Mac or browser session is signed into the same iCloud account as the iPhone
  • The reminder syncs to the iPhone
  • The IFTTT app is installed on the iPhone Once connected you’re ready to start creating using the iOS reminders service from the Applet composer or enabling pre-made Applets from the iOS reminders service page. See below for some popular examples to get you started:

Troubleshooting steps

  • Make sure the required permission is granted for the iOS service, as outlined in the steps above.
  • Ensure you’re using the latest version of the IFTTT app and signed into the same IFTTT account where the Applet is enabled, on your iPhone or iPad.
  • Keep in mind that Applets only trigger for events that happen after they’ve been enabled. For instance, an iOS Photos Applet using the “Any New Photo” trigger will only run for photos taken after the Applet is turned on—it won’t retroactively process photos taken earlier.

How to connect Klaviyo to IFTTT

What is IFTTT?

Trusted by millions worldwide, IFTTT (If This, Then That) is a user-friendly automation platform that connects apps, devices, and services without requiring any coding. It works through Applets—customizable automations that link Triggers (“if this”) to Actions (“then that”), enabling seamless automation across different platforms. With a vast range of supported services, IFTTT allows users to create personalized automations tailored to their needs.

How to connect Klaviyo to IFTTT

To start creating Klaviyo automations using IFTTT, you will first need to connect your Klaviyo account to your IFTTT account.

To connect IFTTT from your Klaviyo account, follow the instructions below.

  • Click Install on the IFTTT app listing in the Klaviyo App Marketplace

  • Review the requested permissions and then click Allow To connect Klaviyo from your IFTTT account, follow the instructions below.

  • Go to ifttt.com

  • Click Connect Klaviyo

  • Sign into your Klaviyo account

  • Review the requested permissions on this screen and then click Allow

  • You will be redirected to the Klaviyo service page on IFTTT. A banner confirming the service is connected will briefly appear on screen once the connection is complete.

How to configure and use Klaviyo in your IFTTT Applets

Once connected, you can start creating or enabling Applets that use the Klaviyo service’s Triggers, Queries, and Actions.

To learn how to create Applets, please see our guide How do I create Applets?

For example, to automatically save new profiles to Google Sheets:

  • Navigate to ifttt.com/create
  • Click Add to the right of “If This”
  • Search for and select the Klaviyo service
  • Select the card titled New Profile then press Create Trigger
  • Next, click Add to the right of “Then That”
  • Search for and select the Google Sheets service
  • Select the card titled Add Row to Spreadsheet
  • Configure the fields as per your preference then press Create Action
  • Follow the remaining prompts to save your Applet Once enabled, any time a new profile is created in Klaviyo it will also be added to your Google Sheet.

You can follow these steps to create any Applet with Klaviyo that you’d like.

You can also discover and enable ready-made Applets created by other services and IFTTT users on the Klaviyo service page and the IFTTT Explore page.

How to disconnect the IFTTT Klaviyo service

  • Navigate to ifttt.com
  • Click Settings in the top right
  • Click Remove service next to your connection to disconnect the service Please note, disconnecting the Klaviyo service from your IFTTT account will remove any Applets that use the Klaviyo service.

Any Klaviyo data within IFTTT will be removed according to our terms and conditions at ifttt.com.
