How to get started using IFTTT with Telegram

Background

Telegram is a messaging service that can be connected to IFTTT to automate tasks. With the IFTTT Telegram service, you can create Applets using Telegram Triggers to monitor messages sent to channels or groups. Telegram can also be used as an Action to send messages automatically to channels or groups via the IFTTT Telegram Bot. This guide explains how to connect your Telegram account to IFTTT and get started using it with channels and groups.

Connecting to Telegram

To get started using Telegram’s Triggers and Actions, the first step is to connect to the IFTTT Telegram service. This will add the IFTTT Telegram bot to your account. Follow these steps to connect:

  • Go to the Telegram service page on IFTTT and press Connect
  • Click Start Bot
  • A message from the IFTTT bot will appear prompting you to connect. Select Start, then choose Authorize IFTTT.
  • You’ll be redirected back to the IFTTT Telegram service page, where a banner will confirm that the service has been successfully connected. Once connected, you’ll be able to add the IFTTT bot to Telegram groups and channels for use in Applets. See the sections below for steps on adding the bot to groups and channels.
Using Telegram Groups with IFTTT

After connecting the Telegram service, you’ll need to add the IFTTT bot to the specific Telegram group you want to use. The bot must be made an administrator so it can read or send messages as part of an Applet. Follow the steps below to add the bot to a group and grant the required permissions.

  • Click the group name at the top of the chat to open Group settings
  • Select Add members, then add IFTTT (bot) to the group
  • Select Edit, then Administrators, and promote the IFTTT bot to a group administrator
  • Return to the group chat and type /start@ifttt
  • Select the /connect_group option
  • The bot will reply confirming that the group has been connected
  • Go to ifttt.com to create Applets using this Telegram group
Using Telegram Channels with IFTTT

After connecting the Telegram service, you’ll need to add the IFTTT bot to the specific Telegram channel you want to use. The bot must be made an administrator so it can post messages or monitor activity for use in an Applet. Follow the steps below to add the bot to a channel and grant the required permissions.

  • Click your channel name at the top of the chat to open Channel settings
  • Select Admins, then choose Add Admin
  • Add IFTTT (bot) as an admin
  • Click Edit in the top right of the channel settings
  • If the channel is private, set it to Public. Please note, the IFTTT Telegram service only works with public channels
  • Set a custom URL for the channel, then select Done
  • Open Invite Links and copy your channel’s share link
  • Open your private chat with the IFTTT bot and select Start, or type /start
  • Send the /connect_channel command
  • Paste your channel’s share URL
  • The bot will reply confirming that the channel has been connected
  • Go to ifttt.com to create Applets using this Telegram channel
Having trouble after creating an Applet?

If you’ve connected Telegram but your Applet isn’t working as expected, see our Troubleshooting Common Telegram Issues guide. It walks through common scenarios and how to resolve them.

How to Remove Meta Service Connections from IFTTT

You can disconnect your Meta accounts from IFTTT at any time by following the steps in this guide. When you remove a service, any Applets using that service will also be removed. Additionally, the disconnected account will no longer be linked to IFTTT, and IFTTT will no longer be able to access data related to that account to run your Applets.

Removing a Facebook Profile from IFTTT

  • Go to Facebook settings on IFTTT.
  • You’ll see a list of Facebook accounts connected to IFTTT.
  • Click Remove next to the account you want to disconnect.
  • The account will be disconnected from IFTTT.

Removing a Facebook Page from IFTTT

  • Go to Facebook Pages settings on IFTTT.
  • View the connected Facebook Pages accounts.
  • Click Remove next to the account you wish to disconnect.
  • The account will be removed from IFTTT.

Removing Facebook Lead Ads from IFTTT

  • Go to Facebook Lead Ads settings on IFTTT.
  • Identify the connected Facebook Lead Ads accounts.
  • Click Remove to disconnect the selected account.
  • The account will no longer be linked to IFTTT.

Removing Instagram from IFTTT

  • Go to Instagram settings on IFTTT.
  • You’ll see a list of Instagram accounts connected to IFTTT.
  • Click Remove next to the account you want to disconnect.
  • The account will be disconnected from IFTTT.

Removing Threads from IFTTT

  • Go to Threads settings on IFTTT.
  • View the Threads accounts connected to IFTTT.
  • Click Remove to disconnect the selected account.
  • The account will no longer be linked to IFTTT. Once you remove a service connection, you will need to connect again from the relevant service page if you wish to use it with IFTTT again in the future.

How to Send Texts with IFTTT

IFTTT makes it easy to automate SMS messages, whether you want to receive important alerts, send reminders, or manage customer communication. If you’ve ever wondered, “Can IFTTT send a text?”, the answer is yes! There are multiple ways to do it.

From the IFTTT SMS service to Android automation and third-party integrations, this guide will walk you through each option and help you choose the best method for your needs.

Sending Texts to your phone with the IFTTT SMS Service

IFTTT’s SMS service allows you to send text messages to your own phone. This is ideal for personal notifications that require immediate attention.

When to the IFTTT SMS Service:

The SMS service is best for high-priority alerts, such as:

  • Security alerts – Receive a text when your home security camera detects motion or your alarm system is triggered.

  • Time-sensitive reminders – Get a text when a meeting is about to start or when it’s time to take medication.

  • Smart home monitoring – Be notified when a water leak sensor is triggered, a door is left open, or your thermostat reaches a specific temperature.

  • Finance tracking – Receive an SMS when a large transaction occurs in your bank account via a finance app integration such as the Monzo service’s Any card purchase above an amount trigger. How to connect to the IFTTT SMS service:

  • Go to the IFTTT SMS service page and press Connect

  • Enter your phone number, then press Send PIN

  • You’ll receive an SMS message with a confirmation code, enter that in the 4 digit PIN field then press connect Once connected, you can enable Applets via the SMS service page or create your own using the Applet composer. Keep in mind that this service is limited to sending texts to the phone number associated with your IFTTT account.

Sending Texts to Others with the Android SMS Service

The IFTTT SMS service can be used to send texts to your own phone. However, if you have an Android device, you can use IFTTT’s Android SMS service to send text messages to any number. This requires an Android phone with the IFTTT app installed and SMS permissions enabled.

When to Use the Android SMS Service:

The Android SMS service is great for automating SMS messages from your personal phone to your contacts, such as:

  • Location-based messages – Automatically send a text when you enter or leave a specific area, such as notifying someone that you arrived home safely.

  • Meeting reminders – Send a text a day before an appointment or event to remind attendees and confirm their attendance.

  • Customer follow-ups – Automatically send a thank-you text when a prospective customer fills out a form, such as a SurveyMonkey survey.

  • Lead generation – Send a follow-up text when someone submits a form from your lead ad, such as a Google Ads form submission: Google Ads on IFTTT. How to connect to the Android SMS service:

  • Install the IFTTT app on your Android phone.

  • Got the Android SMS service page on IFTTT.

  • Click Connect

  • Enable SMS permissions in the app settings.

    • On your Android Device, go to settings > Apps > IFTTT > Permissions > SMS > then set to Allow.

Additional SMS Service Options

For businesses, bulk messaging, or sending SMS from a number other than your own several third-party SMS providers integrate with IFTTT. These services allow you to send texts to another number but may require a separate subscription and additional fees.

Available SMS Services:

  • ClickSend – Send and receive SMS globally using the ClickSend platform. Integrate SMS into anything. Also supports MMS, Fax, Post, Voice and Email.
  • SMS & Text-To-Speech by seven – Send SMS and issue Text-To-Speech calls via the seven SMS Gateway - a Germany based business established 2003.
  • GatewayAPI SMS – IFTTT lets you connect GatewayAPI SMS with hundreds of the most popular apps, so you can automate your work and have more time for what matters most — no code required!

Start Automating SMS Messages with IFTTT

Whether you want to receive instant alerts, send automated reminders, or manage business messaging, IFTTT makes it easy to automate SMS communication.

  • For personal alerts, use the IFTTT SMS service.
  • To send texts from your Android phone, connect the Android SMS service.
  • For business or bulk messaging, explore ClickSend, seven, or GatewayAPI. Start automating today and make sure your most important messages always get delivered.

How to tell if a service requires a Pro or Pro+ plan to use

Background

When creating an Applet, you might see a message saying that it uses features that require a Pro or Pro+ subscription. This usually means the Applet includes a Trigger, Query, or Action that isn’t available on the Free tier.

Most services on IFTTT offer Triggers and Actions you can use on the free plan. However, some services include Triggers and Actions that require a Pro or Pro+ subscription to access. Queries are only available on the Pro+ tier.

This guide explains how to check whether a service or a specific Trigger or Action requires a subscription, and where to look for that information when you’re creating an Applet.

From the Service Page

You can confirm whether a service’s Triggers and Actions require a subscription by visiting its service page on IFTTT. A service page be accessed by typing the name of the service into the search bar in ifttt.com/explore then pressing the relevant service card.

Once on the service page, scroll down to the section that lists the service’s Triggers, Queries, and Actions. Each item will show whether it is available on the Free, Pro, or Pro+ tier.

Here’s how to read the badges:

  • No badge shown
  • Triggers and Actions without a badge are available on all tiers, including Free.
  • In the example below from the Weather Underground service, none of the Triggers or Actions include a Pro or Pro+ badge, so they can be used on any plan:
  • Pro badge
  • Triggers and Actions with a Pro badge require an IFTTT Pro subscription.
  • In the example below from the X (Twitter) service, the Pro badge indicates these options need a Pro or Pro+ plan:
  • Pro+ badge
  • Triggers and Actions with a Pro+ badge require an IFTTT Pro+ subscription.
  • In the example below from the Google Ads service, the Pro+ badge indicates they are only available on Pro+: Checking the service page is the most direct way to see what services are available with your current plan.

From the Applet Composer

You can also see which features require a subscription while creating or editing an Applet.

After selecting a service in the Applet composer, you’ll see the list of available Triggers, Queries, and Actions. Each item will display a badge if it requires a Pro or Pro+ subscription.

Here’s how to read the badges:

  • No badge shown
  • When building an Applet, Triggers and Actions without a badge are available on all tiers.
  • In the example below, the options shown do not include a Pro or Pro+ badge, so they can be used on any plan:
  • Pro badge
  • If a Trigger or Action displays a Pro badge in the composer, it requires an IFTTT Pro subscription.
  • In the example below, the Pro badge indicates these options need a Pro or Pro+ plan:
  • Pro+ badge
  • Triggers and Actions marked with a Pro+ badge require an IFTTT Pro+ subscription.
  • In the example below, the Pro+ badge indicates these options are only available on Pro+: This is helpful if you notice a badge while selecting a Trigger, Query, or Action during Applet creation and want to know whether your current plan allows you to use it.

Identifying Published Applets That Require a Subscription

Some published Applets available on the Explore page include Triggers, Queries, or Actions or features that require a Pro or Pro+ subscription to enable.

For more information about published Applets that require a subscription and how to identify them, see this guide: What is a Pro/Pro+ Applet?

How to use the AI Content Creator service

We’ve just launched three new AI-powered services, designed to save you time and increase your productivity.

In this article, we’ll take a look at the AI Content Creator service.

How does the AI Content Creator service work?

The AI Content Creator service offers 5 queries, powered by GPT-3, to help you draft content, outline blog posts, and come up with post ideas and titles.

These queries, combined with triggers and actions from almost 800 different services available on IFTTT, offer endless ways to automate your workflow and kiss writer’s block goodbye!

How to create an AI Content Creator Applet

To get started, create or log in to your IFTTT account, then head to ifttt.com.

Click If This, and select your trigger.

In this example, we’ll select the Note widget - Any new note trigger, which fires when you take a new note with the Note widget on your mobile device.

Next, click the plus button ( + ), click Query, and select your AI Content Creator query.

We’ll go with the AI Content Creator - Draft content on a topic query here.

For the Topic, click Add ingredient, and select the NoteText ingredient.

For the Tone, we’ll go with Professional.

Finally, click Then That, and select your action.

In this example, we’ll go with the WordPress - Create a post action.

We’ll leave the Title as NoteText, replace the Body field with the Content ingredient from the AI Content Creator query, and set the Post status to Save as draft.

Example use cases

With almost 800 services on IFTTT, the possibilities for AI-powered Applets are practically infinite. Here are a few favorites from the IFTTT team:

Rate limit

The new AI-powered services share a rate limit of 200 queries per day.

This means, for example, that you can make 100 queries with the AI Content Creator service, 50 queries with the AI Social Creator service, and 50 with the AI Summarizer service before hitting your daily limit.

If you hit the limit, your AI Applet runs will be skipped until the limit resets the following day.

Subscription requirements

The AI Content Creator service uses queries, which are currently only available on the Pro+ plan.

To use the AI-powered services on IFTTT, consider upgrading to Pro+.

How to Use the InOut WhatsApp IFTTT Service

The InOut WhatsApp Receive/Send Message IFTTT service is a convenient way to interact with Applets using WhatsApp. You can trigger Applets by messaging the InOut bot, and you can receive notifications from the bot as an Applet Action. Triggers let you start an Applet using WhatsApp, while Actions let you receive updates for things like social media activity, smart home device activity, and more.

Connecting to the InOut WhatsApp Service

To get started, the first step is connect your IFTTT account to the WhatsApp Receive/Send Message Service. You can do so by following these steps:

  • Log in to your IFTTT account, then navigate to the WhatsApp Receive/Send Message Service page
  • Press the Connect button
  • Enter the Phone number used with your WhatsApp account in the Phone field, then press continue
  • You’ll then receive a message from the InOut.bot’s number via WhatsApp with a one-time code. Enter this code in the code field, then press authenticate
  • Select ‘Connect account to IFTTT’, then you’ll be returned to the InOut WhatsApp service page. From there, you can begin enabling or creating Applets using the service.

Using InOut WhatsApp Service’s Triggers

The InOut WhatsApp service supports two Triggers:

  • You send any WhatsApp message to InOut: This Trigger fires when you send any message to the InOut Bot on WhatsApp.
  • You send a specific WhatsApp message to InOut: This Trigger fires when you send a specific message to the InOut Bot on WhatsApp. All other messages are ignored. These Triggers can be used to start an Applet via WhatsApp. For example, you can send the bot a message to trigger an Applet that turns on a smart device in your home, such as a Phillips Hue bulb.

You can follow these steps to create an Applet using an InOut service Trigger:

  • Go to ifttt.com/create
  • To the right of If This, search for “InOut WhatsApp Receive/Send Message” and select the service from the results.
  • Select the InOut Trigger of your choice, then press Create Trigger
  • Next, press Add to the right of Then That, search for a service of your choice, then complete the prompts to add your Action
  • Follow the remaining prompts to name and save your Applet Now, whenever you send a message to the InOut.bot’s WhatsApp number (+1 234 332 2110), your IFTTT Applet will run.

Using InOut WhatsApp Service’s Action

The InOut WhatsApp service supports one Action:

  • Send me a message: This Action sends a WhatsApp text message to your number from the InOut.bot WhatsApp number. This Action provides a useful way to receive notifications via WhatsApp. For example, you could be notified when a camera detects motion, when a user posts a Tweet on X, or when a smart device changes state.

You can follow these steps to create an Applet using an InOut service Action:

  • Go to ifttt.com/create
  • To the right of If This, search for a service of your choice, then complete the prompts to add a Trigger to your Applet
  • Next, press Add to the right of Then That, search for “WhatsApp Receive/Send Message” and select the service from the results.
  • Select the card titled Send me a message, then configure the Message field to your preference based on the message content you want to receive when the Applet runs.
  • Follow the remaining prompts to save your Applet With the above setup, whenever your Applet’s trigger event occurs, the InOut.bot will send you a WhatsApp message.

Check your InOut.Bot Balance

While Triggers (messages you send to the InOut bot to trigger an Applet) are free and unlimited, there are limits on the use of Actions (messages the InOut bot sends to you). Their pricing page explains the available subscription tiers and the included balance for each: inout.bot.

These plans are separate from IFTTT Pro or Pro+ subscriptions and are managed directly by InOut.bot.

You can check your balance by doing the following:

  • Open WhatsApp
  • Start a chat with the InOut.Bot using the Bot’s number: +1 234 332 2110
  • Send the message: Balance
  • The bot will reply with your balance and instructions to add more messages if needed New users of InOut.bot are given a free trial balance of 20 messages before a subscription is required.

Key Reminders When Using the InOut.bot Service

Here are a few important points to remember before using the InOut.bot:

  • IFTTT connects to InOut.bot, not WhatsApp directly. That means the automation happens through InOut.bot’s platform.
  • Your Applets will only work with messages to and from InOut.bot. You won’t be able to use this to text your other WhatsApp users directly through IFTTT. All messages must go through the InOut.bot number.
  • If your Applet that uses an InOut Action is running, but you are not receiving a message from the bot, and your activity feed shows:
    • “Your balance of Messages to Send is 0. You can subscribe now to keep sending messages to your number.”
  • This indicates your InOut account has no remaining message balance. Follow the steps in the section above to add more message balance.

How to use the Monzo 1p Savings Challenge on IFTTT

What is the Monzo 1p Savings Challenge?

The Monzo 1p Savings Challenge is a simple way to build savings automatically throughout the year using IFTTT.

Starting on January 1st, the Applet moves money from your main account into a Pot of your choice each day. The amount increases by 1p every day:

  • Day 1: 1p
  • Day 2: 2p
  • Day 3: 3p
  • and so on… Once enabled, the Applet handles the daily movement into your chosen Pot automatically.

How to get started

  • Go to the Mozno Service page
  • Click Connect
  • Follow the on-screen prompts to connect your Monzo account to IFTTT
  • Once connected, enable the Monzo 1p Savings Challenge Applet here After enabling the Applet, money will begin moving from your main account into your selected Pot automatically based on the calendar date

Can I pause the challenge?

Yes. You can stop the Applet at any time and then resume it later. To do so:

  • Go to your My Applets page.
  • Click or tap the 1p Savings Challenge Applet to open it
  • Select Connected and toggle the slider to switch it to Disconnected While disconnected, the Applet will not run. When you’re ready to resume you can re-enable the Applet by viewing it here and pressing connect.

Important to note:

When you re-enable the Applet, it resumes based on the current calendar date, not where you previously left off.

For example:

  • If you enable the Applet on January 10th, it will move 10p into your chosen Pot.
  • If you disable it on January 11th and re-enable it on January 20th, it will move 20p on that day, not 11p. This keeps the challenge aligned with the calendar date.

How do I stop the Applet?

If you no longer want to continue the savings challenge:

  • Go to your My Applets page.
  • View the 1p Savings Challenge Applet.
  • Toggle Connected to Disconnected. Once disconnected, the Applet will no longer run.

Is the Monzo 1p Savings Challenge available on the free tier?

Yes! The Monzo 1p Savings Challenge Applet and other Applets published by Monzo are available on all IFTTT plans and does not require a paid subscription.

Although this Applet includes filter code, which is typically part of the Pro+ tier, it is available for free as part of the partnership between Monzo and IFTTT.

Explore more Monzo Applets

Looking for a different way to save?

You can explore more Applets from Monzo on the Mozno Service page

For example, you can try the Reverse 1p Savings Challenge, which starts by moving a larger amount at the beginning of the year and gradually decreases each day.

Browse the full collection on the Monzo page to find an approach that fits your savings style.

How to use your connected service

Most services can be used as either a trigger, query, or action, which represent the fundamental building blocks of an IFTTT Applet. Some services may not provide triggers, queries, and actions - if there’s a trigger, query, or action you’d like to use but isn’t available, be sure to reach out to the service provider to request this!

To make use of these building blocks, you need to create an Applet by using the Applet composer (which can also be accessed by clicking or tapping “Create” when logged into IFTTT).

Using your service as a trigger

To use your service as a trigger, open the Applet composer and click the Add button next to ‘If This’:

Select your preferred service from the list, or use the search bar to filter results. Then choose from a list of the available triggers. If you have not already connected to the service, you may be redirected to do so as explained in our help article Connecting to a service.

Some services may ask you to select a device or input other values before confirming the trigger.

Using your service as a query

After you’ve added a trigger, click the plus button, and then select Add query. Please note, to use queries you’ll need to have a Pro+ subscription.

You can now select your preferred query service from the list, or use the search bar to filter results. Then choose from a list of the available queries. If you have not already connected to the service, you may be redirected to do so as explained in our help article Connecting to a service.

Some services may ask you to select a device or input other values before confirming the query. If your trigger provides ingredients, you can use these in the query step.

Using your service as an action

After you have added your trigger service, select Add next to ‘Then That’.

Select your preferred action service from the list, or use the search bar to filter results. Then choose from a list of the available actions. If you have not already connected to the service, you may be redirected to do so as explained in our help article Connecting to a service.

Some services may ask you to select a device or input other values before confirming the action. You can also use ingredients from previous steps in your action field.

Using a premade Applet

  • Connect to your service as explained in our help article Connecting to a service on IFTTT
  • Visit the service page and select a premade Applet
  • Click Connect

How to use Zoom with IFTTT

Zoom can be used as either a trigger, query, or action, which represent the fundamental building blocks of an IFTTT Applet.

To make use of these building blocks, you need to create an Applet by using the Applet composer. This can be done by clicking or tapping “Create” when logged into IFTTT or by connecting to a premade Applet on the Zoom service page.

Using Zoom as a trigger

To use the Zoom service as a trigger, open the Applet composer and click the Add button next to ‘If This’:

Select Zoom from the list or use the search bar to filter results. Then, choose from a list of available triggers. If you have not already connected to the Zoom service on IFTTT, you’ll receive a prompt to link your Zoom account. Follow the on screen prompts to complete the connection flow, then you’ll be able to continue with settings up your Applet. The help article, Connecting Zoom to IFTTT, can help you connect Zoom.

Using Zoom as a query

After you’ve added a trigger, click the plus button, and then select Add query.

Select Zoom from the list or use the search bar to filter results. Then, choose from a list of available queries. If you have do not have Zoom connected, you may be redirected to do so.

Using Zoom as an action

After you have added your trigger service, select Add next to ‘Then That’.

Select Zoom from the list or use the search bar to filter results. Then, choose from a list of available actions. If you have not already connected to Zoom, you may be redirected to do so.

You will be asked to enter the following values to create a meeting:

  • The name of the meeting
  • The date and time of the meeting
  • The meeting length
  • An optional meeting Agenda You may use values from previous steps of the Applet to populate the action fields.

Using a premade Zoom Applet

  • Connect to Zoom as explained in our help article, Connecting Zoom to IFTTT

  • Visit the Zoom service page and select a premade Applet

  • Click Connect or

  • Connect to Zoom as explained in our help article, Connecting Zoom to IFTTT

  • Visit the Explore page

  • Search for Zoom and select a premade Applet

  • Click Connect

SMS Content Guidelines for IFTTT Applets

When using the IFTTT SMS service, the Send me an SMS action lets you send a message directly to your phone. This is useful for staying notified about important events, such as upcoming meetings, motion detected by cameras, new leads, and more.

To ensure messages conform to carrier guidelines, any SMS sent through IFTTT Applets must follow our content guidelines. Applets that generate messages with prohibited content may be disconnected or removed.

Restricted SMS Content

Messages should not include content that:

  • Relates to illegal activities in your geographic location. This includes, but is not limited to:
    • Cannabis: Messages about cannabis products, even if terms, images, or links are not explicitly included.
    • CBD: Messages about CBD products where prohibited.
    • Prescription Medication: References to prescription medication that cannot legally be sold over-the-counter.
  • Contains hate speech, harassment, exploitative or abusive language, or originates from hate groups.
  • Is fraudulent or intended to deceive.
  • Contains malicious content, such as malware, viruses, or harmful links. Following these guidelines helps ensure your Applets continue working reliably.

Troubleshooting SMS or Phone Call PINs not being received

Background

When connecting the SMS or Phone Call services, IFTTT sends a PIN to the phone number you enter as part of the connection process. The SMS service delivers this PIN by text message, while the Phone Call service delivers it through an automated call.

The Phone Call service Connection Screen

In some cases, the PIN does not arrive because your phone is blocking messages or calls from unknown numbers. The steps below can help you identify and adjust these settings on both iOS and Android devices.

Please note: The SMS service is included with IFTTT Pro and Pro+, on the free plan you won’t be able to connect to the SMS service or use SMS Applets.

SMS PIN not being received

To connect to the IFTTT SMS service, go to ifttt.com/sms and press Connect. Enter your phone number, then press Send PIN.

If you do not receive the SMS PIN, your phone may be filtering or blocking messages from unknown senders. Follow the steps below to troubleshoot:

iOS
  • Open Settings
  • Select Messages
  • Scroll down to the Unknown Senders heading and check:
    • Screen unknown senders: If this is turned on, your device may move the PIN message into a separate list or block it entirely.
    • Filter Spam: If enabled, your device may be inadvertently blocking the message. Try temporarily turning these settings off, then request the PIN again.

If you receive the PIN, the message was likely being filtered. Add the IFTTT SMS number to your contacts, then re-enable your desired filtering in the Messages settings.

Android

(Android settings vary by device and manufacturer, but the steps below cover the most common locations.)

  • Open Messages
  • Select Settings
  • Look for:
    • Spam protection
    • Block numbers and spam
    • Block unknown or private numbers
    • Block messages from unknown senders If any of these are enabled, temporarily disable them, then request the PIN again.

If you receive the PIN, add the IFTTT SMS number to your contacts to prevent future issues. After that you can re-enable your spam filtering settings.

Phone Call PIN not being received

To connect to the IFTTT Phone Call service, go to ifttt.com/phone_call and press Connect. Enter your phone number, then press Send PIN.

If the automated call does not reach your phone, call blocking or call screening settings may be active. Follow the steps below to troubleshoot:

iOS
  • Open Settings
  • Select Phone
  • Scroll down and check the following:
    • Under the Silence Unknown Callers heading:
      • If Silence or Ask Reason for Calling are enabled, your phone may send the IFTTT call directly to voicemail.
    • Under the Call Filtering heading:
      • Unknown Callers: If enabled, this may move the call to the unknown callers list, preventing you from receiving it.
      • Spam: If enabled, this setting may inadvertently block IFTTT’s call. Try turning these settings off, then request the call again.

If the call comes through, add the IFTTT call number to your contacts, then re-enable your preferred settings.

Android

(Android settings vary by device and manufacturer, but the steps below cover the most common locations.)

  • Open the Phone app on your Android device.
  • Tap the three‑dot menu or More icon, then select Settings.
  • Look for the following options:
  • Caller ID & spam / Spam and Call Screen
    • If your phone shows a Caller ID & spam or Spam and Call Screen section, open it.
    • Turn See caller and spam ID on if it is off. Google Help
    • If available, turn Filter spam calls off temporarily. This setting may send suspected spam calls to voicemail or block them before they reach you. Google Help
  • Blocked numbers
    • In Phone app > Settings > Blocked numbers, check whether unknown or unidentified callers are blocked. If any of these are turned on, temporarily disable them and try connecting again.

If the PIN call comes through, add the IFTTT phone number to your contacts. After that you can re-enable your desired call filtering settings.

Still having trouble?

If you have confirmed that your phone is not blocking messages or calls and you still do not receive the PIN, contact our support team for help.

Using IFTTT with AI Assistants

IFTTT connects to AI assistants like Claude and ChatGPT, letting you control your connected services and automations through natural conversation. Instead of switching between apps, just ask your AI assistant to do it for you.

Getting Started

Requirements
  • A free IFTTT account (Pro or Pro+ unlocks additional features)
  • A supported AI assistant (Claude Pro, ChatGPT, or another MCP-compatible client)
How to connect IFTTT to your AI assistant
  • Find IFTTT in your AI assistant’s settings
    • In Claude: Go to Settings > Connectors, then search for IFTTT
    • In ChatGPT: Go to Settings > Connected Apps, then search for IFTTT
    • If your AI assistant asks for a server URL, enter: https://ifttt.com/mcp
  • Authorize IFTTT — You’ll be redirected to IFTTT to sign in and grant permission
  • Connect your services — Make sure the services you want to use (e.g., Philips Hue, Google Sheets, Spotify) are connected on your My Services page
  • Start using it! — Try asking “What can I do with IFTTT?” to explore

What You Can Do

Once connected, your AI assistant can help you with all of these IFTTT capabilities:

Control your smart home and connected devices

Run actions on any of your connected services in real time — no need to open another app.

  • “Turn off my bedroom lights”
  • “Set my thermostat to 72 degrees”
  • “Send me a notification that says ‘Time to leave for the airport’”
  • “Add a row to my Google Sheet with today’s expenses”
Discover services and what they can do

Explore the 1,000+ services IFTTT supports and find out what’s possible.

  • “What services does IFTTT have for smart home?”
  • “What can I do with Philips Hue through IFTTT?”
Find and enable Applets

Search IFTTT’s library of pre-built Applets and turn them on without leaving the conversation.

  • “Find Applets for saving Spotify tracks to a spreadsheet”
  • “Enable the Applet that texts me when the ISS passes overhead”
Create custom Applets

Describe an automation in plain language and your AI assistant will help build it as an IFTTT Applet.

  • “Create an Applet that emails me when it’s going to rain tomorrow”
  • “Set up an automation that logs my Strava workouts to Google Sheets”
Connect new services

If a service isn’t connected yet, your AI assistant can guide you through connecting it to IFTTT right from the conversation.

Troubleshooting

Authentication problems

Issue

Solution

“Unauthorized” or “Invalid token” error

Disconnect and reconnect IFTTT in your AI assistant’s settings. Your authorization may have expired.

Authorization page doesn’t load

Try clearing your browser cache, or use a private/incognito window to re-authorize.

Can’t find IFTTT in your AI assistant

Make sure your AI assistant supports MCP connectors. If it asks for a server URL, enter https://ifttt.com/mcp.

Service connection issues

Issue

Solution

“Service not connected” error

Visit My Services on IFTTT and connect the service you’re trying to use.

Action runs but nothing happens

The service connection may be stale. Go to the service’s settings page on IFTTT, disconnect it, and reconnect it.

“Service temporarily unavailable”

The third-party service may be experiencing an outage. Wait a few minutes and try again.

Other issues

Issue

Solution

“Upgrade required”

Some features require an IFTTT Pro or Pro+ subscription. Visit IFTTT Plans to upgrade.

“Missing required fields”

The action needs more information. Try being more specific — for example, specify which light or which spreadsheet.

Responses seem slow

Some actions take a moment to complete, especially if they involve multiple services. If requests consistently time out, try again in a few minutes.

Frequently Asked Questions

What are the requirements?
  • A free IFTTT account (Pro or Pro+ unlocks additional features)
  • A supported AI assistant (Claude Pro, ChatGPT, etc.)
  • The services you want to control must be connected to your IFTTT account
Does this cost extra?

The IFTTT MCP integration works with a free IFTTT account. Some advanced features (like multi-action Applets and filters) require a Pro or Pro+ subscription. Your AI assistant may also require its own subscription.

How is this different from using the IFTTT website or app?

The IFTTT website and mobile app let you browse, create, and manage Applets through a visual interface. The AI assistant integration lets you do the same things through natural conversation. It’s especially useful for:

  • Quick actions — “Turn off the lights” is faster than opening an app
  • Discovery — Ask “What can I automate with my Google Calendar?” instead of browsing
  • Complex automations — Describe what you want in plain language and let the AI help build it
What happens to my data?
  • Your AI assistant sends requests to IFTTT on your behalf using a secure authorized connection
  • IFTTT processes requests the same way it would from the website or app
  • Your IFTTT password is never shared with the AI assistant
  • IFTTT’s Privacy Policy and Terms of Service apply
  • Refer to your AI assistant’s privacy policy for how it handles conversation data
Which AI assistants are supported?
  • Claude by Anthropic
  • ChatGPT by OpenAI
  • Microsoft Copilot Studio
Can I use multiple AI assistants at the same time?

Yes. Each AI assistant creates its own connection to your IFTTT account. You can use IFTTT with Claude, ChatGPT, and others simultaneously.

What services work through AI assistants?

Any service connected to your IFTTT account can be used through the AI assistant integration. IFTTT supports over 1,000 services including smart home devices, productivity tools, social media, and more. Browse the full list at ifttt.com/services.

What is MCP?

MCP (Model Context Protocol) is an open standard that lets AI assistants connect to external tools and services. It’s the technology that enables your AI assistant to interact with IFTTT on your behalf. You don’t need to know anything about MCP to use IFTTT with your AI assistant — just connect and start asking.

Webhooks service FAQ

What is the Webhooks service?

The Webhooks service allows you to integrate other services on IFTTT with your DIY projects via simple web requests.

The Webhooks service uses realtime Triggers, so Webhook Applets normally run within a few seconds.

Where do I find my Webhooks key?

A Webhooks key will be generated after connecting to the Webhooks service. To connect, go to the Webhooks service page, then press Connect Webhooks.

Once connected, head to the Webhooks service page, scroll to the About Webhooks section located at the bottom of the page then click Documentation, or click this link to view the Webhooks Documentation page.

Where can I find my Applet’s Webhook URL?

If your Applet uses a Webhooks Trigger, the easiest way to find its Webhook URL is to view the Applet and scroll to the bottom of the page. There, you’ll see a field labeled Your webhook URL. Your Applet’s specific URL will be shown there, and you can click or tap it to copy it.

When using the Receive a web request Trigger, the Webhook URL will follow this format:

[maker.ifttt.com](https://maker.ifttt.com/trigger/{event}/with/key/{webhooks_key})

In this URL, {event} is the event name configured in your Trigger’s Event Name field, and {webhooks_key} is your account’s Webhooks key.

If you’re using the Receive a web request with a JSON payload Trigger, the URL follows a similar format, but includes an additional /json path element:

[maker.ifttt.com](https://maker.ifttt.com/trigger/{event}/json/with/key/{webhooks_key})

How do I pass values to Webhooks?

You can pass up to three values to the Webhooks - Receive a web request Trigger with either a JSON body:

{ "value1" : "", "value2" : "", "value3" : "" }

or x-www-form-urlencoded data:

[maker.ifttt.com](https://maker.ifttt.com/trigger/{event}/with/key/{webhooks_key}?value1=value1&value2=value2&value3=value3)

These values can then be used in Query Fields and Action Fields by adding the Value1, Value2, and Value3 Ingredients with the Add Ingredient button.

The Webhooks - Receive a web request with a JSON payload Trigger accepts JSON, and requests should include the header Content-Type: application/json:

curl -X POST -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d '{"this":[{"is":{"some":["test","data"]}}]}' [maker.ifttt.com](https://maker.ifttt.com/trigger/{event}/json/with/key/{webhooks_key})

This payload can then be used as an Ingredient in supported Query Fields and Action Fields via the JSON payload Ingredient.

What if I need to pass more than three values?

The Receive a web request Trigger supports the Value1, Value2, and Value3 Ingredients, while the Receive a web request with a JSON payload Trigger supports the JSON Payload Ingredient.

If you need to pass up to three values for use in your Applet, the Receive a web request Trigger is usually the best option. Those values do not need to be parsed and can be added directly to your Applet using the corresponding Ingredients in Query Fields and Action Fields.

If you need to pass more than three values, you can use the Receive a web request with a JSON payload Trigger instead. That Trigger allows you to send the full JSON body as a single Ingredient, which can then be parsed with filter code and used in the relevant Action fields.

How do I add custom headers to my Webhooks Action?

The Webhooks - Make a web request Action has an optional field for custom headers. Any custom header is acceptable, except Content-Type, which is set in the Content Type field.

An example of how the custom headers field might look:

From: biffmcifttt@example.com
Some-Header: someCustomValue; someOtherValue
Warning: Over 9000

The Mozilla Developer Network has a list of standard HTTP headers, but any key/value pairs will work in the Additional Headers field.

Can I use IPv6 addresses in the URL field?

You sure can! IPv6 addresses should conform to certain standards:

  • Like any other URL, they must start with a protocol (http://or https://)

  • The IPv6 address itself must be [surrounded by brackets].

  • Like any other URL, they can include a port (:1234) and a pathname (/my/awesome/path). Examples of valid IPv6 URLs:

    http://[FEDC:BA98:7654:3210:FEDC:BA98:7654:3210]:80/index.html https://[1080:0:0:0:8:800:200C:417A]/index.html http://[3ffe:2a00:100:7031::1]

Can I publish an Applet with Webhooks?

Yes, Webhook Applets can be published. Check out our Applet Publishing Guide for suggestions.

What if I want to build a service on IFTTT?

If you’re interested in building a service on IFTTT, head to the Developer Dashboard and become a partner.

Is there a fixed range of IP addresses for Webhooks?

No, IFTTT does not set a fixed IP or range of IPs for Webhooks or any other services.

Which plans include access to Webhooks?

The Webhooks service’s two Triggers and one Action are available on the Pro tier. The Webhooks service also supports three queries that are available on the Pro+ tier. The webhooks service is currently not available on the free tier.

How do I get support for Webhooks?

If you are struggling to Trigger your Webhooks Applets, the first troubleshooting step we always recommend is simply regenerating your Webhooks key. To do so, head to ifttt.com and click Regenerate key. If applicable, update your Webhooks URLs to use the new key, then perform a few tests.

If you’re still having trouble, we recommend contacting our support team for assistance.

For outbound webhooks, you can also check out our Troubleshooting outbound webhooks help article.

What are Webhooks?

Webhooks are automated HTTP requests sent from one system to another when a specific event occurs. They enable real-time data transfer and can trigger actions in connected applications. This allows systems to communicate and automate processes efficiently.

How do Webhooks work?

Webhooks work by having one application (the sending application) send an HTTP request—usually a POST request—to another application (the receiving application) when a specific event occurs. To set up a webhook, the receiving application provides a unique URL, known as the webhook endpoint, to the sending application.

When the specified event happens—like a new user signing up or a payment being processed—the sending application automatically makes an HTTP request to that URL. This request includes data about the event in the body of the request. The receiving application then processes the data and performs an action based on it, such as updating records, triggering a notification, or starting another process.

Webhooks are one-way, meaning the sending application only communicates when the event occurs. This eliminates the need for the receiving application to constantly check for updates.

Some platforms, like IFTTT, Google Apps Script, and Slack, provide user-friendly interfaces to set up webhooks. More advanced users can manually send webhook requests using tools like the terminal or command-line utilities such as curl.

Using webhooks on IFTTT

Using webhooks on IFTTT allows you to connect and automate actions with services that aren’t directly supported by the platform. By sending a simple HTTP request to a unique webhook URL, you can trigger custom Applets and create more flexible, personalized automations.

How to set up a webhook Applet

Here’s a step-by-step guide to setting up a webhook Applet on IFTTT, whether you want to trigger an Applet using a webhook or send a webhook to an external endpoint.

Setting Up a Webhook Trigger (Receiving a Webhook)
  • Connect to the Webhooks Service:
    • Log in to your IFTTT account.
    • Search for the Webhooks service and click connect.
  • Create a New Applet:
    • Click Create and press Add to the right of If This to set-up your Applet’s trigger.
    • Select Webhooks as the service and choose Receive a web request as the trigger.
    • Give the event a name (e.g., new_user or payment_received). This will be used in the webhook URL (the endpoint).
  • Define the Action:
    • Click Then That and choose an action service (like sending a notification, posting to social media, or updating a spreadsheet).
    • Configure the action as needed.
  • Find Your Webhook URL:
    • After clicking finish in the Applet setup, you will see your webhook URL on the following page, under “your webhook URL”
    • If you need to find it later, the URL will follow this format:
    • https://maker.ifttt.com/trigger/{event_name}/with/key/{your_key}.
      • Replace {event_name} with the name chosen in Step 2.
      • Replace {your_key} with your webhook key.
      • NB: the the above URL does not include /json/after the event name.
        • When using the Receive a web request trigger /json/ will not be included in the URL, vs when using Receive a web request with a JSON payload /json/will be included after the event name.
  • Trigger the Webhook:
    • You can manually trigger the Applet by pasting the webhook URL into your browser and hitting Enter.
    • Alternatively, configure an external service like Google Apps Script or Slack to send an HTTP POST request to the URL, which will trigger the Applet.
Setting Up a Webhook Action (Sending a Webhook to an External Endpoint)
  • Create a New Applet:
    • Click Create and choose the service you want as the trigger (e.g., receiving an email, a calendar event, or a smart light activation).
  • Select Webhooks as the Action:
    • For the action step, choose the Webhooks service and select Make a web request.
  • Configure the Webhook Request:
    • Enter the external endpoint URL where the webhook should be sent.
    • Choose the method (typically POST).
    • Specify the content type (like application/json or application/x-www-form-urlencoded).
    • Add any required data in the body of the request.
  • Complete and Test the Applet:
    • Finalize the Applet setup and test it by performing the trigger event.
    • Check the external system to confirm that it received the webhook correctly.

What is a JSON payload?

When selecting Webhooks for your trigger, you’ll see the following options:

A JSON payload is a structured format used to send data in an HTTP request. JSON (JavaScript Object Notation) organizes data into key-value pairs, making it easy to transfer complex information like user details, event data, or nested objects. For example:

{
  "username": "biffmcifttt",
  "email": "biff@ifttt.com",
  "order": {
    "id": "1234",
    "total": 49.99
  }
}
  • Use “Receive a web request” for simple data passed as URL parameters (e.g., ?value1=abc&value2=123).
  • Use “Receive a web request with a JSON payload” when sending structured, complex data that needs to be processed in actions or filter code.
  • Similar to before, after clicking finish in the Applet setup when creating an Applet using a receive a web-request with JSON payload trigger, you will see your webhook URL on the following page, under “your webhook URL”
  • If you need to find it later, the URL will follow this format:
  • https://maker.ifttt.com/trigger/{event_name}/JSON/with/key/{your_key}.
    • NB: the the above URL does include /json/after the event name.
      • When using the Receive a web request trigger /json/ will not be included in the URL, vs when using Receive a web request with a JSON payload /json/will be included after the event name. For more details on handling and parsing JSON data in an Applet, check out our article: Parsing a JSON Body with Filter Code.

Troubleshooting Webhooks Applets

In some cases, your webhook key may become outdated. Once you’ve ensured that the URL is correct, and your Applet is still not triggering, you can try regenerating your webhooks key. To do this, head to ifttt.com/maker_webhooks/settings and click regenerate key.

For webhook actions that are not working, refer to our guide on Troubleshooting Outbound Webhooks.

  • Check Your Webhook URL:
    • Go to My Applets and click on the relevant Applet. The URL is listed under “Your webhook URL.”
  • Test from the webhooks documentation page
    • Applets that use a webhooks trigger can also be tested from the Webhooks documentation page available here.
    • To test, simply enter the event name for the Applet you’d like to test then press test it. You can also include test data.
      • The To trigger an Event with an arbitrary JSON payload section can be used to test Applets that are using a Receive a web request with a JSON payload trigger
      • The To trigger an Event with 3 JSON values section can be used to test Applets that are using a Receive a web request trigger
  • Regenerate Your Webhook Key:
    • If the Applet isn’t triggering, your webhook key may be outdated.
    • Visit ifttt.com/maker_webhooks/settings and click Regenerate Key.
    • Note: This will invalidate all previous webhook URLs, so you’ll need to update the key in any external services using outbound webhooks.
  • Check Outbound Webhooks:
    • If webhook actions aren’t working, refer to our guide on Troubleshooting Outbound Webhooks.

Summary and Conclusion

Webhooks are a powerful way to automate workflows by enabling applications to communicate through simple HTTP requests. On IFTTT, setting up webhook triggers and actions allows you to connect with external systems, expand automation possibilities, and handle complex data using JSON payloads.

For more information on using webhooks and troubleshooting common issues, visit our Webhooks service FAQ.

What is the multiple accounts per service feature?

This feature gives Pro+ users the ability to connect and manage multiple accounts for certain services.

How to add another account

You can view, edit, and add accounts by going to the Settings page for the service, or during Applet creation.

Add an account while making an Applet

To add a new account when you are in the process of creating an Applet, select the Add Account button under the account dropdown. This can be done during the trigger or action steps for any Applet.

This will open a new window and provide you with the option to log in or create an account for the service you want to connect. Once you have authenticated the new account, you will be given the option to toggle between accounts in the dropdown menu.

The first account added for each service becomes the primary account, meaning that your Applets will default to this account unless otherwise specified.

Add, remove, or reconnect an account

You can manage your connected accounts by going to the Settings page for the service itself. There, you will be able to see all of the accounts that have been authenticated and connected to your IFTTT account. You can choose to Remove, Reconnect, or Add a new account from this page.

Why does it say Default?

The first account you sign into and authenticate becomes your default account. If you create or enable an Applet using that service, this will be the account that is used unless you specify otherwise.

Unsupported services

Non-authenticated services like RSS Feed, Location, and Weather Underground cannot have multiple accounts associated with them because they do not require a login or associated account.

Additionally, the following services do not yet support multiple accounts:

  • Air-Q
  • BlueLink Smart
  • Cielo Home
  • Discord
  • Disec Tronic
  • Email
  • Epion
  • Google Assistant
  • Group Location
  • Home Connect Wine Cooler
  • HiDrive

  • Webhooks

  • SmartHome AC

Where can I find my Webhooks Key

Background

When you connect the Webhooks service to your IFTTT account, a unique Webhooks key is automatically generated for you. This key acts like a personal identifier that allows external services or devices to securely trigger your Applets that use Webhooks as the Trigger.

Your Webhooks key is what links incoming web requests to your IFTTT account. For example, you might use it to have a device, script, or third-party service send a request to IFTTT to turn on your lights, post a message, or log data in a spreadsheet.

It’s important to note that your Webhooks key is only used with Webhooks Triggers, not with Actions. Since your key uniquely identifies your account, you should keep it private and avoid sharing it publicly. Anyone who has access to your key could trigger your Applets without permission.

Finding your Webhooks key
  • Go to the Webhooks service page.
  • If you haven’t connected it yet, select Connect Webhooks.
  • Once connected, return to the Webhooks service page and scroll down to the About Webhooks section at the bottom.
  • Click Documentation. You can also go directly to the Webhooks Documentation page.
  • Your unique Webhooks key will be displayed at the top of that page
Using your key in a Webhooks URL

If your Applet uses the Receive a web request trigger, your Webhooks URL should be formatted as:

https://maker.ifttt.com/trigger/{event}/with/key/{webhooks_key}

If your Applet uses the Receive a web request with a JSON payload trigger, your Webhooks URL should be formatted as:

https://maker.ifttt.com/trigger/{event}/json/with/key/{webhooks_key}

(Note the /json/ after the event name.)

In the above URLs:

  • {event} should be replaced with the event name configured in the Event Name field of your Applet’s Trigger
  • {webhooks_key} should be replaced with your Webhooks Key
Finding the Webhooks URL for a specific Applet

You can also find your Applet’s exact Webhooks URL by:

  • Opening the Applet.
  • Scrolling to the Your Webhook URL field.
  • Copying the URL directly from that field. For additional details on how to pass values to Webhooks in your Webhooks Triggers you can view our FAQ here.

Which locations are Supported by the IFTTT SMS Service?

The IFTTT SMS Service Details

The IFTTT SMS service lets you interact with your Applets using text messages. You can use it in two ways:

  • Trigger an Applet by sending a text message to IFTTT’s number. This can be useful for quick actions, like logging information or controlling smart devices.

  • After you’ve connected to the IFTTT Sms service, you can find the phone number to text to trigger your Applet by visiting ifttt.com/sms/settings. The number will be listed under the IFTTT Phone Number heading.

  • Send me an SMS as an action in an Applet. This allows you to get alerts, reminders, or notifications directly to your phone. The SMS service is available on Pro and Pro+ plans and includes:

  • 100 messages per month for users in the U.S. and Canada.

  • 10 messages per month for users in other supported locations.

IFTTT SMS Service Availability

Please note that some carriers outside of the US are not supported yet. You can find the full list of supported locations below.
