  • Add a trigger and at least one action to your Applet
  • Click the + sign between your trigger and action:
  • Then click the + sign to the right of Filter code:
  • Once in the filter code editor you can choose one of the three options in the Code generators section:
  • On the following prompt, configure the fields based on how you’d like your filter code to run, then click copy to clipboard:
  • Lastly, paste the code into the filter code editor, then click add filter in the top right:
  • Follow the remaining prompts to save your Applet If you need help creating filter code for a use case that isn’t covered by the Code generators, you can open a ticket with our friendly support team who will be happy to assist with creating your code.

Get random strings from an array with no duplicates

Getting random strings from an array can be useful when posting to several different platforms with one Applet, while ensuring each of your posts is unique.

The below filter code will post a different notification to Facebook, Twitter, and Reddit when you go live on Twitch:

let postContent = [...]

  let shuffled = [...postContent].sort(() => Math.random() - 0.5);
  let [pickFacebook, pickTwitter, pickReddit] = shuffled;

  FacebookPages.createLinkPage.setMessage(pickFacebook);
  Twitter.postNewTweet.setTweet(pickTwitter);
  Reddit.submitLinkReddit.setTitle(pickReddit);

This code will pick one of the strings from the postContent array at random for each of your actions. Then it will compare the posts chosen for Facebook and Twitter, forcing Twitter to pick again until they are no longer the same. Finally, this is repeated for Reddit, checking against the Facebook and Twitter posts and retrying until all three are different.

Note that the above is customized to work with the Twitch - New stream started by you trigger, plus the Facebook Pages - Create a link post, Twitter - Post a tweet, and Reddit - Submit a new link actions. The trigger and actions can all be changed, but you’ll need to update the corresponding ingredients and methods above.

You can also add or change the strings in the postContent array, just be sure to include at least as many options as you have actions.

If you need a hand getting this set up, reach out to IFTTT support and we’d be happy to help!

How can I run an Applet only after sunset?

With IFTTT Pro+ you can add queries to your Applets to add more context, and filter code to ensure your actions only run when certain conditions are met.

In this case, we’ll query the sunset and sunrise times in your location, then use filter code to skip the Applet’s action while the Sun is still up.

Let’s create an example Applet where we want to send an SMS message using the Android SMS service after we arrive home, but only when it’s after sunset.

Start with the Location - You enter an area trigger, add the Weather Underground - Current weather query, and the Android SMS - Send an SMS action.

Now add the following filter code:

let sunrise = moment(Weather.currentWeather[0].SunriseAt);
let sunset = moment(Weather.currentWeather[0].SunsetAt);
let currentTime = Meta.currentUserTime;

let afterSunrise = currentTime.isAfter(sunrise);
let beforeSunset = currentTime.isBefore(sunset);

if (afterSunrise && beforeSunset) {
  AndroidMessages.sendAMessage.skip()
}

This code takes the sunrise and sunset times from the Weather Underground query, plus the user’s current time, and skips the SMS action if the current time is between sunrise and sunset (when the Sun is up).

Note that if you are using this code in an Applet with different actions, you’ll need to replace the second last line, AndroidMessages.sendAMessage.skip(), with the relevant skip code for your action(s).

You can find the skip code for your Applet’s actions below the filter code editor under Actions; the skip code will end in .skip():

How to add filter code

Adding filter code to a new Applet

To add filter code to a new Applet, head to ifttt.com and select a trigger and add at least one action. Now, click the first plus button ( + ) and click Add filter. You can use the generators on the right, or write your own code.

When you’re finished, click Create filter > Continue > Finish, and you should be all set!

For information about adding filter code in the IFTTT mobile app, check out the article Can I add/edit filter code in the IFTTT mobile apps?

Adding filter code to an existing Applet

To add filter code to an existing Applet, head to ifttt.com, select your Applet, and click Settings. Now click the first plus button ( + ) and select Add filter.

When you’re done, click Create filter, then Update, and you should be good to go!

Available methods for your Applet’s trigger, queries, and actions

All available filter code methods for your Applet’s trigger, queries, actions, a couple of methods available to all Applets (Meta.currentUserTime and Meta.triggerTime), and filter code generators will be displayed on the right:

How to create a Google Calendar event only on weekdays or business hours

Setting Long-Term Reminders with Date and Time Features

Sometimes, you need to remember important tasks or appointments far in advance. Whether it’s days, months, or even years ahead, you can use our Date and Time feature in conjunction with Google Calendar to set up timely reminders.

Here’s a practical example:

Let’s say you need to pay your rent at the end of each month. You want a reminder on a weekday, just in case the last day falls on a weekend. Here’s how to set it up:

  1. Go to ifttt.com/create

  2. Click “Add” next to “If This,” then search for and select the Date & Time - Every month on the Trigger. Press ‘Create Trigger’.

  3. Click “Add” next to “Then That,” then search for and select the Google Calendar -> Create a Detailed Event Action.

  4. Use a filter code to skip the date and time falls on a weekday. To add the filter code, click the + sign in between the Trigger and Action.

Copy-paste the filter code below:

let day = Meta.currentUserTime.day()

if (day === 0 || day === 6) {
GoogleCalendar.addDetailedEvent.skip()
}
  1. Hit ‘Save Applet’.

This combination allows you to create a recurring reminder that will notify you on a weekday at the end of each month. You’ll get a timely heads-up for your rent payment, helping you stay on top of your financial responsibilities.

By using these features, you can create custom reminders for various recurring tasks, from bill payments to important anniversaries or regular check-ins. This way, you’ll never miss a beat, no matter how far in advance you need to plan.

Parsing JSON body with filter code

The Webhooks - Receive a web request with a JSON payload trigger allows you to set up Applets that take the JSON body from a web request and put it in a single ingredient {{JsonPayload}}.

You can parse the JSON with filter code to build conditions based on the data or split it up into individual action fields.

As an example, let’s send a web request with the following body:

curl -X POST -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d \
'{
 "user": {
   "name": "Ben",
   "email": "ben@example.com",
   "status": "Platinum",
   "customer_since": "6/10/2013"
 }
}' \
[maker.ifttt.com](https://maker.ifttt.com/trigger/new_customer/json/with/key/my_secret_key)

Our goal is to populate individual cells in a Google Sheet with the name, email, etc. using the Google Sheets - Add row to spreadsheet action.

However, if we simply set the Formatted row field to {{JsonPayload}}, all data will be added to a single cell in the spreadsheet.

Instead, we can parse the JSON with the JSON.parse method, adding each value to a separate cell with the following filter code:

let payload = JSON.parse(MakerWebhooks.jsonEvent.JsonPayload)

let formattedRow = `${payload.user.name}|||${payload.user.email}|||${payload.user.status}|||${payload.user.customer_since}`

GoogleSheets.appendToGoogleSpreadsheet.setFormattedRow(formattedRow)

Each JSON body is unique and can have many different keys, values, or even arrays, so you’ll need to modify this code depending on the structure of your web request’s JSON body.

The {{JsonPayload}} ingredient is (none)

Note that the URL for the Webhooks - Receive a web request with a JSON payload trigger is in a slightly different format than the regular IFTTT Webhook URL (https://maker.ifttt.com/trigger/{event}/with/key/{key} vs https://maker.ifttt.com/trigger/{event}/json/with/key/{key}).

If the URL of your web request does not include /json/ as above, the {{JsonPayload}} ingredient will be empty:

Getting started guide

How do I check my Applet activity and troubleshoot issues?

If you’d like to view a history of your Applet runs or check the activity for a specific Applet, you can do so through the activity feed. This is a helpful way to see when an Applet last ran, whether it was successful, and if any errors occurred.

This article explains how to view your Applet’s activity, understand what different messages mean, and troubleshoot issues.

Accessing the activity feed

To see activity across all Applets, go to ifttt.com/activity. You can also press your profile icon and select Activity. This page shows a feed of recent Applet runs along with any new service connections.

Each Applet also has its own individual activity feed. To view the activity for a specific Applet:

  • Go to ifttt.com/my_applets or open the IFTTT app and tap My Applets.
  • Press the Applet you want to review.
  • Select View activity. You’ll see a list of events showing the last time the Applet ran, what triggered it, and whether it completed successfully. If something went wrong, the activity feed will usually indicate what happened and whether the issue came from the Trigger, Query, or Action.

Viewing the activity feed

When viewing the activity feed, you’ll see a list of Applet runs in chronological order, with the most recent at the top. Each entry shows the status of the Applet’s Trigger, Query, and Action, along with timestamps for when each step occurred.

To the right of each step, you’ll see a dropdown icon. Pressing this icon expands the entry to show more details about that part of the Applet:

When expanded, you can review the specific Ingredients passed from the Trigger and any Queries, as well as the field values used in the Action. This can be especially helpful for troubleshooting issues or understanding exactly how your Applet ran.

Trigger type

At the top of each Applet’s activity feed, you’ll see a message indicating the type of Trigger it uses:

  • “Successful Trigger checks” means the Applet is using a polling Trigger.
  • “Listening for Trigger notifications” means the Applet is using a real-time Trigger. Understanding whether an Applet uses polling or real-time Triggers can provide helpful context. It can give you a sense of how quickly the Applet should run after a Trigger event, and it can also be useful when troubleshooting issues.

You can learn more about the difference between polling and real-time Triggers here: What is the difference between polling Applets and realtime Applets.

Troubleshooting tips

If your Applet isn’t running as expected, here are some steps you can take to diagnose and resolve the issue.

Check the Activity Feed

Start by viewing the Applet’s activity feed. If something went wrong, you’ll see a message such as “Trigger failed,” “Query failed,” or “Action failed”, depending on where the issue occurred. Press the dropdown arrow next to the relevant step to expand the details. An error message may be shown that explains what went wrong and which part of the Applet encountered the issue.

This can give you a clear idea of whether the problem lies with the service providing the Trigger, the data returned by the Query, or the service handling the Action.

Check the Ingredients used in the Applet

If a Trigger or Query doesn’t return the expected data, it could cause the Action to fail. You can expand the details for that portion of the Applet run in the activity feed to view exactly what data was passed in each Ingredient.

If you see “Action failed”, this can sometimes be due to missing or incorrectly formatted data coming from a Trigger or Query. For example, if your Action expects a URL but the Ingredient passed is empty or contains unexpected text, it may not complete successfully. Review your Applet’s settings to confirm the correct Ingredients are mapped to the appropriate fields.

Reconnect the Service

Reconnecting the service connected to the failed step can often resolve the issue.

If the problem occurred in the Trigger, go to the Trigger service’s page, select Settings, and then choose Reconnect.

Similarly, if the error happened during the Action, follow the same process on the Action service’s page.

Consider Polling Frequency

If your Applet uses a polling Trigger, IFTTT checks for updates at set intervals:

  • Every 5 minutes on IFTTT Pro and IFTTT Pro+
  • Once an hour on the Free plan Sometimes these checks may fail due to issues like a service’s API not responding in time. When that happens, you’ll see a failed trigger check in the activity feed.

If a Trigger event occurred during that failed check, the Applet will run at the next successful check. If no event occurred, the Successful Trigger checks heading will show that the next check succeeded with no new events found.

If a Trigger Event hadn’t occurred around the time of the failed check then its best to think of these failed checks as IFTTT being temporarily unable to check if your Applet should run at that time, not that it missed a event it should have run for.

Contacting support

If you’re still having trouble after trying the above steps and you’re on an IFTTT Pro or IFTTT Pro+ plan, you can reach out to our support team for help. For guidance on how to contact support, please visit: How to contact support

Previous Section: Managing services on IFTTT | Next section: Staying connected

How do I connect, disconnect, and manage services on IFTTT?

Each Applet depends on one or more services to power its Triggers, Queries, or Actions. This section covers how to connect services, manage them, disconnect ones you no longer need, and take advantage of features like multi-account support.

Connecting services

When you enable or create an Applet, IFTTT will prompt you to connect any required services that you haven’t connected yet. You can also connect a service directly from its service page. Just press Connect and follow the prompts to authorize IFTTT’s access.

Each service page includes:

  • Applets – A list of Applets that use the service and can be enabled
  • My Applets – Applets you’ve enabled or created that use the service
  • Details – A full list of the service’s available Triggers, Queries, and Actions. You can press any item to see what fields it includes and what Ingredients are available for Triggers and Queries Once connected, a service will stay linked until you remove or update it.
Updating the connected account

To switch which account is connected to a service (for example, to log in with a different Google or Twitter account), go to the service page, press Settings, then select Reconnect. Log in with the new account you want to use. Unless you’re adding a new multi-account connection, all of your Applets using that service will now use the updated login.

Multi-account support

If you’re a Pro+ user, some services support connecting more than one account. This is useful if you manage multiple social media accounts, work across multiple personal and professional accounts, or otherwise need to connect multiple accounts to a specific IFTTT service.

To add multiple accounts to a single service, after connecting the first account:

  • Go to the service page
  • Press Settings in the top right
  • Press Add account.
  • Sign in with the additional account. You can then choose which account to use each time you create or edit an Applet.

Disconnecting services

If you want to remove a service from your account, go to its service page and press Settings, then select Remove.

  • If the service was used in any Applets you created, those Applets will be archived. They won’t run but can be restored later.
  • If the service was used in a published Applet you enabled, that Applet will be permanently deleted from your account. You can always enable it again later from the service page or from ifttt.com/explore.

Viewing your connected services

To see all the services currently connected to your account:

  • Visit ifttt.com/my_services, or
    • On web, click your profile icon in the top right and choose My Services, or
    • In the app, tap the profile icon in the bottom right and select My Services If your profile icon shows a red exclamation mark, it means there’s an issue with a connected service. This usually happens when a service’s permissions expire or due to a credentials change.

An example of an account with an issue with a connected service

To fix it:

  • Tap or click the service name in My Services
  • Press Settings
  • Choose Reconnect and follow the on the screen prompts Once reconnected, any affected Applets should resume working.

How do I create an IFTTT account?

Getting started with IFTTT is easy. First, you’ll create an account, which gives you access to thousands of automations. The section covers account set up and the different plans that are available.

Creating an account

To get started with IFTTT, head to ifttt.com/join and sign up.

You can create an account using an email and password or sign in with Google, Apple, or Facebook for a quicker setup.

The IFTTT sign up page

Signing up using Email

To create an account using your email and a password, enter your email address, choose a password, and select Get started. Your account will be created and linked to the email address you provided.

To log in on another device or return to your account later, go to ifttt.com/login and enter your email and password.

Signing up using Google, Apple, or Facebook Single Sign On

IFTTT also supports signing up using Google, Apple, or Facebook through Single Sign-On (SSO). To create an account this way, select the appropriate Continue with button.

For example, choosing Continue with Google will link your account to your Google login. If you’re not already signed in to Google, you’ll be prompted to do so.

To log in on another device, simply select the same Continue with option you used during sign-up.

Personalize your account

After creating your account, visit ifttt.com/settings to personalize your experience. You can update your username, add a profile picture, set your time zone, and enable two-factor authentication (2FA) for added security. We strongly recommend enabling 2FA to help protect your account.

If you signed up using Google, Apple, or Facebook, you can also add a password to your account from the settings page. This allows you to log in using your email and password in the future, even if you originally signed up with Single Sign-On.

Plan options: Free, IFTTT Pro, and IFTTT Pro+

Signing up for IFTTT is completely free and gives you access to core features that help you get started with automation right away. If you need additional features, you can also upgrade to IFTTT Pro or IFTTT Pro+ plan. Both IFTTT Pro and IFTTT Pro+ offer a 7 day free trial if you’d like to try them out before subscribing.

The Free plan is a great way to explore what IFTTT can do. You can create up to two Applets at a time, either build your own or use ones shared by the community. Applets run at standard speeds, and you’ll have unlimited Applet runs, simple no-code tools, and full access to the IFTTT mobile app.

IFTTT Pro is a good fit if you’re ready to do more with your automations. You can create up to 20 Applets and unlock advanced features like multi-action Applets. You’ll also get faster Applet speeds, access to exclusive service such as Webhooks and Twitter, and support from our customer service team.

IFTTT Pro+ gives you the most flexibility and control. It includes everything from Pro, plus unlimited Applets, access to AI services, the ability to connect multiple accounts across services, and advanced tools like queries and filter code so you can fine-tune how your Applets run. You’ll also receive priority support when you need help.

To compare plans or choose the one that works best for you, visit ifttt.com/plans.

Previous Section: How to get started with IFTTT | Next section: Enabling Applets

How do I create Applets?

If you can’t find a published Applet that fits your needs or simply prefer to build your own, you can create one from scratch. This section will walk you through how to create Applets, add advanced features, and publish them to share with other users.

Creating Applets

At its most basic, an Applet includes a Trigger and one Action. The Trigger is what initiates the Applet, and the Action is what happens when the Applet runs.

To create an Applet, go to ifttt.com to access the Applet composer.

If you’re on ifttt.com, you can also click the Create button in the top right corner. Or if you’re using the IFTTT mobile app, you can tap the Create icon in the center of the bottom navigation bar.

The ifttt.com Applet Composer

The first step is to add a Trigger to your Applet. To do this, click Add next to If This. Then, search for the service you want to use as your Trigger and select it from the list under the Available Services heading.

Next, you’ll see a page with the available Triggers for that service. Choose the one you’d like to use, fill out the required fields to customize it to your preferences, and press Create Trigger.

Now that your Trigger is added, the last step is to add an Action. Press Add to the right of Then That and search for the service you want to use for your action then select an action of your choice.

When configuring your Applet’s action field ingredients from the Trigger can be used. Ingredients are data from your Applets Trigger that can be passed to the action. Depending on the Trigger used, ingredients could be the content of a Facebook post, the time the Trigger occurred, or the name of the device that triggered the Applet.

In this example, we’re using the Date and Time service, which includes the CheckTime ingredient. This ingredient passes a timestamp of when the Trigger occurred. For instance, when adding a Send me an email action to this Applet, the CheckTime ingredient can be used in the Subject and/or Body fields of the action.

To add an ingredient from your Applet’s Trigger, click the Add ingredient button below the relevant action field, then select the name of the ingredient you’d like to include.

When the above Applet runs, it’ll send an email that says “It’s [CheckTime], don’t forget rent is due in two days!” with [CheckTime] being replaced with the date and time the Applet ran.

Once your Applet’s Action is configured, press Create Action then press continue. You can then edit your Applet’s title then press Finish to complete creating your Applet. One thing to note, Applet titles are used as a reference to what they do but won’t affect how they run.

After pressing finish your Applet will automatically be enabled and run next time the conditions set in the Trigger are met.

Adding advanced Applet features

Applets can include advanced features like Queries, delays, filter code, and multiple Actions to customize how they run. When an Applet runs, it follows this order: Trigger, then Queries, filter code, delay, and finally Actions.

Queries

Queries are best thought of as a way to request additional information when your Applet runs. Like Triggers, Queries provide ingredients that can be used in your Applet’s filter code and Actions. Queries are available when on the IFTTT Pro+ plan.

For example, the Google Calendar service includes a List Events for a Date Query that returns all events scheduled on a specific date. This could be used with the Date and Time service’s Every day at Trigger and a Slack Action to send a daily recap of upcoming meetings. Since the Date and Time Trigger doesn’t include calendar data, the Query is what provides that information for use in the Slack message via the Applet’s Action.

A Query can be added to an Applet by selecting the plus (+) button below the Trigger, then choosing the plus (+) button next to Query. Once added, ingredients from the Query can be added to Action fields using the Add ingredient button.

Filter Code

Filter code allows conditional logic to be added to an Applet. Information from the Applet’s Trigger and any Queries can be used to control how the Applet runs, such as skipping Actions or updating Action field values based on specific conditions.

For example, an Applet might use a location Trigger to turn on a light when someone arrives home. However, that Action may only be needed between 9 p.m. and midnight. In that case, filter code could be used to add time-based logic so the Applet skips the Action outside of those hours.

Another example might involve skipping an Action to turn on the air conditioning if a Query to a window contact sensor shows that a window is open.

Filter code is available on the IFTTT Pro+ plan. Filter code can be added to an Applet by selecting the plus (+) button below the Trigger, then choosing the plus (+) button next to Filter code. For a step-by-step guide, see the Help Center article on Building with filter code.

Delays

Delays can be used to postpone when an Applet’s Action or Actions are run. A delay takes place after the Trigger, any Queries, and filter code, but before the Actions are executed.

Delays are available on all IFTTT plans. To add one, press the plus (+) button below the Trigger, then press the plus (+) button next to Delay. Enter the desired delay time and press Apply to continue.

Delays can range from as short as 15 seconds to as long as three hours. If a delay is needed between multiple Actions, that can be done by linking separate Applets together. For more details, see this guide.

Multiple Actions

Multiple Actions can be added to an Applet so that several tasks are performed when it runs. All Actions will run when the Applet is triggered, after Queries, filter code, and any delay.

To add multiple Actions, first add your initial Action. Then, press the plus (+) button below that Action to add another. You can continue adding as many Actions as needed.

Multiple Actions are available on the IFTTT Pro plan

Publishing Applets

Once you complete the setup and press Finish, your Applet will be activated and ready to run whenever its Trigger conditions occur. By default, it will only be visible to you, so other users won’t be able to find it on ifttt.com/explore.

If you want to share your Applet and make it discoverable by others, you can choose to publish it. Publishing is optional and does not affect how your Applet runs. For a detailed guide on publishing Applets, see this section.

Previous Section: Enabling Applets | Next section: Managing Applets

How do I manage Applets

Once an Applet is created or enabled, it can be managed from your My Applets view. This section covers how to view, edit, archive, and restore Applets, as well as how to access activity history and manage published Applets.

Viewing Your Applets

All of your enabled and disabled Applets are listed on your My Applets view. You can use the search bar at the top of the page to quickly find a specific Applet by name.

If you’re on the Free or Pro plan, you’ll also see a counter next to All that shows how many Applets you currently have vs the total number of Applets available on your plan. Any Applet listed in the All tab counts toward your plan’s Applet limit. If you no longer need an Applet you can archive or delete it at which point it will no longer count towards the limit.

The Applet counter is highlighted above in yellow

Enabled Applets will display a slider marked Connected, meaning they will run next time a Trigger Event occurs. Disabled Applets appear grey with a slider displaying Connect, indicating they are inactive and will not run until re-enabled.

Managing Individual Applets

To manage a specific Applet, click its card to open the Applet detail page. From there, you have several options:

Enable or Disable the Applet
  • If the Applet is currently enabled, the slider in the top right will say Connected. Click or tap it to disconnect the Applet, the slider will move left and show Connect, meaning it’s now disabled and will no longer run.
  • If the Applet is disabled, press Connect to enable it again. The slider will move to the right and show Connected.
View Activity

Press View activity to see a log of when the Applet has run. This includes detailed information about each Trigger event and resulting Action. The activity feed section of this guide covers viewing the activity feed and troubleshooting issues in more detail.

Editing your Applet

To update how the Applet works, press Settings in the top right. This will open the Applet composer, where you can modify the Trigger, Actions, and other configuration details.

You can also change the Applet’s title directly from its detail page. Updating the title won’t affect how the Applet functions, it’s just for your own reference.

Archive or Delete the Applet

At the bottom of the Applet detail page, you’ll see an option to Archive or Delete, depending on the Applet:

  • Archive: If you created the Applet, you’ll see the option to archive it. This removes the Applet from your My Applets view and stops it from running. It won’t count toward your Applet limit, and you can restore it at any time.
  • Delete: If you enabled a published Applet created by someone else, you’ll see the option to delete it. This permanently removes it from your account and it can’t be restored. However, you can enable it again at any time by visiting ifttt.com/explore. Applets that have been deleted will no longer count toward your Applet limit.

Published Applets

If you’ve created and published Applets for other users, they’ll appear under the Published tab to the right of All on the My Applets page. Click this tab to view and manage your published Applets. Selecting an Applet lets you review and update its settings.

Archived Applets

Applets you’ve archived can be found under the Archive tab next to My Applets. If you’d like to use an archived Applet again:

  • Click the Applet in the Archive view.
  • Press Restore to return it to your My Applets list.
  • Press Connect if you’d like it to begin running again. Previous Section: Creating Applets | Next section: Managing services on IFTTT

How to get started with IFTTT

What is IFTTT?

IFTTT is a powerful automation platform that helps you connect your favorite apps, devices, and services to work together seamlessly. Whether you want to automate social media posts, control smart home devices or streamline productivity tasks, IFTTT makes it easy to create custom automations. No coding is required.

What can I do with IFTTT?

With IFTTT, you can connect over 1,000 different apps and devices, including Twitter, Dropbox, Evernote, Fitbit, and Google Assistant, to automate tasks and streamline your digital life. On IFTTT, we call these integrations services. By linking services together, you can create Applets. These automations help your apps and devices work in new and powerful ways. Whether it’s cross posting across different social media platforms, controlling smart home devices, or receiving important alerts, IFTTT makes automation simple and accessible. Explore all IFTTT services here.

How to get started

This guide will walk you through everything you need to get started, including creating an account, enabling Applets, building your own automations, and exploring advanced features.

The bottom of each page in this guide includes a link to the next section. If you prefer to skip ahead, you can use one of the links below.

What are Applets and how do I enable them?

Now that your account is set up, you can start creating and enabling Applets.

What are Applets?

Applets are automations that connect two or more services on IFTTT. Services are the basic building blocks of IFTTT. Some example services are Facebook, Twitter, Fitbit, and Gmail.

At its most basic an Applet requires a Trigger and an Action. A Trigger is the data that, when changed, prompts an Applet to run. An Action is the work that IFTTT initiates when a trigger fires.

Some example Triggers include Twitter - New tweet by you and WordPress - Any new post. Some examples of actions are WordPress - Create a post and Twitter - Post a tweet.

You can build your own Applets using available Triggers, Queries, and Actions, or enable Applets created by the IFTTT community. For example, you might automatically cross-post new Tweets to your Discord server or use a location Trigger to turn on your porch light when you return home.

This section covers how to enable community-created Applets.

Finding Applets

Services and other users can publish Applets for anyone to enable. These published Applets serve as premade templates, making it easy to set up the automation that fits your use case. To find Applets, visit ifttt.com/explore and use the search bar to look for what you’re interested in. You might describe your use case or type in the name of a service, for instance, “link Spotify to YouTube” to find related Applets.

Performing a search on ifttt.com/explore

In the screen grab above, the top row of cards links to service pages where you can learn more about each service. Below them, the larger cards represent Applets that you can enable. You can also search for a specific service by name and click its card in the top row to view more Applets published for that service.

The TP-Link Kasa Service page, with published Applets that can be enabled

Applet details

When viewing the Applet cards, each will show details that will tell you more about what the Applet does.

In the example above:

  • Service icons are located in the top left show which services the Applet uses. This one uses both the Date & Time service and Twitter, so both icons appear.
  • The title in the center gives a short description of what the Applet does.
  • The publisher is listed just below the title. This tells you who created and published the Applet. In this case, it was published by the official Twitter service on IFTTT. For other Applets, you might see an individual IFTTT username, meaning it was created and published by a user for others to enable.
  • In the bottom left, you’ll see the number of users who have enabled the Applet.
  • In the bottom right, a tier badge may appear, indicating if the Applet requires a Pro or Pro+ plan to enable. If there’s no badge, anyone can use it regardless of their plan. In the example above, a Pro badge is shown. That’s because the Twitter service is only available to IFTTT Pro and IFTTT Pro+ users, so this Applet wouldn’t be available on a free account.

Enabling Applets

Once you’ve found an Applet that catches your eye, click or tap on it to view more details, then press “Connect” to enable it. Depending on the Applet’s settings, you may be prompted to fill in specific fields, such as choosing a playlist, to tailor how the Applet runs.

If you haven’t connected a service the Applet uses before, you’ll need to authenticate that service first. Simply follow the on-screen prompts to link the relevant account to IFTTT before enabling your Applet. You can also connect services directly through their service page, which is covered in more detail later in this guide.

After completing these steps, your Applet is enabled and will run automatically the next time the trigger criteria are met. An Applet only triggers for events that happen after it’s been enabled—it won’t run retroactively. For example, if you enable an Applet with the iOS Photos service’s “Any new photo” trigger, it will run for photos taken after the Applet is turned on, but not for any taken beforehand.

Leaving Feedback

If you find a service missing a Trigger, Query, or Action that would improve your automations, you can easily suggest it.

On any service page, under the Details tab, you’ll find an option to leave feedback via the Suggest new button. Your suggestions are sent directly to the service’s administrators for consideration in future updates.

Follow Up on Social

  • YouTube: The IFTTT YouTube channel is packed with tutorials, feature walkthroughs, and use case inspiration. Whether you’re just getting started or looking to build more advanced Applets, you’ll find plenty of helpful content. View IFTTT’s Youtube channel here

Exploring Featured Applets and Services

The IFTTT website and mobile app regularly highlight featured Applets and popular use cases, making it easy to discover new ways to automate your everyday tasks. These recommendations are a great starting point if you’re looking for inspiration or want to see what others are using.

New services are being added to the platform all the time, so it’s worth checking back often. Whether it’s a smart home device, productivity tool, or social media platform, you might find something new that fits perfectly into your routine.

IFTTT mobile apps

About iOS background syncing

What is background syncing?

The iOS services (iOS Photos, iOS Contacts, iOS Reminders, iOS Reading List) require occasional access to your device to run. Whenever you open the IFTTT app, it checks for new data (a new photo, for example).

The IFTTT app also tries to check for new data when you’re not using it. This happens in two different ways: Background App Refresh and location assist syncing.

What is Background App Refresh?

Background App Refresh is an iOS feature that allows apps to run occasionally in the background (even if you don’t open them) to check for new data. Background App Refresh is enabled by default when you install the IFTTT app.

Because the IFTTT app cannot control when a Background App Refresh occurs, these types of background syncs can be unreliable. If you do not open the IFTTT app for a day or two, Background App Refreshes may stop altogether.

What is location assist syncing?

Location assist syncing uses your device’s Location Services to ensure more reliable and consistent background syncs. Any time you visit or leave a location, Location Services runs the IFTTT app in the background to check for new data.

Using Location Services with the IFTTT app has a minimal impact on your device’s battery. You may notice the ➤ icon appear in your device’s status bar. IFTTT does not track your device or use your location unless you use the iOS Location service.

You can enable location assist syncing in the IFTTT app by tapping your profile picture and selecting Sync options.

What happens if I force close the IFTTT app?

If you force close the IFTTT app, Background App Refresh will stop until you open the IFTTT app again. We recommend that you do not force close the IFTTT app.

How do I manage or add new widgets on my device?

Any Applet that using the Button, Note, or Camera services can be a widget. Here’s how to add and manage them.

How to Add widgets on iPhone

Adding widgets from the IFTTT app on iOS:

Applets using one of the above services will appear in the Widgets section under your profile in the IFTTT app. You can visit your profile by tapping the icon on the top right of the IFTTT app.

Using Today View to access Widgets on iOS:
  • From your home screen, swipe right until you reach the Today View
  • Scroll down to the bottom and tap Edit
  • Tap the plus button ( + ) in the top right corner
  • Scroll down and select IFTTT
  • Choose your widget style and tap Add Widget
Accessing Legacy iOS widgets:

Legacy widgets can also be accessed in the Today View:

  • From your home screen, swipe right until you reach the Today View
  • Scroll down to the bottom and tap Edit
  • Scroll down again and tap Customize
  • Find IFTTT and tap the green plus button ( + )
  • Tap Done

How to add widgets on Android

Adding widgets from the IFTTT app on Android:
  • Open the IFTTT app and tap on your widget Applet
  • Next to ‘You may use this widget on your home screen.’, tap Add
    • Note: The instructions you receive for adding the widget to your home screen are handled by the Launcher app, not IFTTT, so you may receive different instructions than below
How to add widgets from the home screen on Android:
  • Long press any empty space on your home screen and tap Widgets
  • Scroll down to find IFTTT, then long-press either IFTTT Large or IFTTT Small and drag onto an empty space on your home screen
  • Select your Applet

How to connect Apple Shortcuts with IFTTT

Apple Shortcuts are a great way to enhance how your Apple devices integrate with the world around you. By utilizing both Shortcuts and IFTTT, you can easily connect and manage automations that simplify your life, boost your productivity, and save you time every day.

What is IFTTT?

Trusted by over 30 million users worldwide, IFTTT (If This, Then That) is a no-code automation platform that connects apps, devices, and services. It works through Applets—customizable automations that link Triggers (“if this”) to Actions (“then that”)—enabling seamless automation across various platforms. With a vast range of supported services, IFTTT allows users to create personalized automations tailored to their needs.

Get started with IFTTT today and unlock a smarter, more connected digital experience with one of the most versatile automation tools available.

What are Shortcuts?

The Shortcuts App is Apple’s native automation program. It allows users to set specific rules for automated sequences, similar to the way IFTTT Applets operate. Additionally, the Shortcuts app provides access to services that don’t natively integrate with IFTTT, such as Siri. By combining Shortcuts and IFTTT, users can extend their automation capabilities, linking the Apple ecosystem with a multitude of third-party devices and platforms.

How to Connect IFTTT to Shortcuts

Create your Applet
  • Open the IFTTT app and tap Create
  • Tap If This, and select the iOS Shortcuts - Shortcut automation started trigger
  • Tap Then That, and select your action
  • Tap Continue
  • Feel free to edit the Applet title, then tap Finish to save your new Applet
Create the iOS Shortcut
  • Open the Shortcuts app and tap the plus button ( + ) in the top right corner
  • Tap Add action > Apps > IFTTT > Trigger Applet
  • Tap the blue arrow ( > ) next to ‘Select an Applet’, then tap Choose
  • Select the Applet you’d like this Shortcut to run (please note that only Applets that use the iOS Shortcuts - Shortcut automation started trigger will appear in this list)
  • Name your Shortcut by tapping Trigger Applet at the top of the screen. This will be the custom phrase you will use to trigger your Applet
Trigger an Applet from a Shortcuts automation

The Automation tab within the Shortcuts app goes beyond Siri and allows you to connect your Applets to many unique functions from your Apple device.

  • Open the Shortcuts app and tap Automation
  • Tap Create Personal Automation
  • Select the trigger of your choice
  • In the Actions section, tap Add Action > Apps > IFTTT > Trigger Applet
  • Select the Applet you’d like this Automation to run (please note that only Applets that use the iOS Shortcuts - Shortcut automation started trigger will appear in this list)
  • Tap Next
  • If available, toggle Ask Before Running off so you do not need to verify each time the Automation runs
  • Feel free to change the name of your Automation, then tap Done

iOS Photos file formats: HEIC vs JPEG

If your iOS device is running iOS 11 or later, your photos may be saved as HEIC files. Unfortunately, most services on IFTTT are not yet compatible with this format.

For example, the Automatically back up your new iOS photos to Google Drive Applet will fail if your images are in HEIC format.

Luckily, it’s possible to switch over to a more widely compatible format (JPEG).

To do so, open the Settings app on your iOS device, tap Camera > Formats, and select Most Compatible.

Now all new photos you take will be saved as JPEG files, and you shouldn’t run into any further compatibility issues with your iOS Photos Applets.

Using Siri with Note widget service

To get started, just say one of the following to Siri:

  • Take a note with an Applet
  • Take a note with I-F-T-T-T Note: Siri doesn’t usually understand the pronunciation of IFTTT (“gift” without the g), so it’s best to spell it out.

What is the Note widget?

Note widget is a service on IFTTT that’s powered by the app. You can connect one trigger to any other action on IFTTT to create a two-step experience for running that action:

  • Write or speak something (the input text)
  • Press the widget button Typically, Note widget Applets will have the input text sent to the action. For example, the “Quickly email yourself a note” Applet sends the input text to the body of an email.

You can learn more about how to use Note widget here or its’ siblings, Button widget and Camera widget.

How do I use Note widget with Siri?

  • Make sure you have at least one Note widget Applet turned on. The “Quickly email yourself a note” Applet is an easy one to try out.
  • The rest is easy. Tell Siri “take a note with an Applet.” The first time you do this, you’ll get the following prompt:
  • Tap Yes to continue.
  • When Siri asks you what you want your note to say, it’s up to you. Whatever you say becomes your input text.
  • If you only have one Note widget Applet, Siri will ask you to confirm its name. If you have more than one, you’ll pick from your list.
  • Once you confirm the Applet, you’re done! IFTTT will take care of the rest.

What happened to the DO mobile apps?

We combined our DO and IF apps into one powerful IFTTT app (iOS / Android). All the functionality of DO is available in IFTTT with the Button widget service, and now you can manage all of your Applets with widgets from one place.

Why does the IFTTT Android app require ‘Allow all the time’ location permissions?

Applets using the Location service require constant access to your device’s location so they can run when you enter/exit your specified area.

However, there are other triggers and actions on IFTTT that also require ‘Allow all the time’ location permissions, like Android Device - Connects to any WiFi network.

While these sorts of triggers and actions may not appear to use location services, starting from Android 10, most apps that perform functions related to WiFi and Bluetooth require constant location access.

For further clarification and technical details, please consult the official Android developer documentation.

Why does the IFTTT app need permission to access functions on my device?

IFTTT can do tons of cool things for you when you use it via the app. To run Applets that use your location, your camera, your contacts, and other smartphone features, we ask for a few permissions:

  • Camera: Permission to access your device’s camera and photos
  • Contacts: Permission access contact details in your address book
  • Location: Permission to access location information when your Applet runs
  • Phone (Android only): Permission to access your phone call logs
  • SMS (Android only): Permission to read your SMS database and to send SMS from your device
  • Storage (Android only): Permission to access your photo database We only access these functions when an Applet you’ve turned on requires access in order to run. You can revoke access to each function and edit or disable your Applets at any time.

Why the IFTTT App shows a persistent notification on Android

You might notice a small persistent notification on your Android device that says the IFTTT app is running in the background. This is part of how the IFTTT app on Android ensures that Applets work correctly.

The notification appears because IFTTT uses a foreground service. This service is required for many types of Applets to run reliably, including those that use triggers from services like Android Device, Android Battery, Location, Group Location, Android SMS and Android Phone Call. By keeping the IFTTT app active, Android makes sure these triggers can detect events and respond without interruption.

While the notification cannot be fully turned off, most devices let you minimize or hide it through system settings. The exact steps vary depending on your phone model and Android version, so check your device’s notification settings if you want to make it less visible.

Manage your account

Data management and privacy

To run your Applets and connections, IFTTT needs to store access tokens that grant us limited access to the accounts you connect to IFTTT. IFTTT only accesses your service accounts when it’s necessary to run the Applets and connections you’ve enabled.

When you sign up for IFTTT, certain information like your email address and time zone is collected. For your convenience, IFTTT uses cookies so you don’t have to sign in every time you access our services.

IFTTT doesn’t sell any personal user data to 3rd parties. IFTTT may use personal data to provide you with suggestions about Applets, connections, or services you might be interested in. At times, IFTTT may aggregate anonymous customer data to better understand our user base.

Our terms of use provides more in-depth information on how we protect your data and privacy.

Do all links run through the ift.tt URL shortener?

By default, yes. If you’d like, you can opt-out:

  • Sign in to your Account settings
  • Go to URL shortening
  • Untick Auto-shorten URLs
  • Save your changes by clicking Update

Emails on IFTTT

Here at IFTTT we have multiple email services, and we also link your account to an email address. Theoretically, a user could have one email address linked to their account, and different emails linked to the Gmail service, the Email service and the email digest service. The following is an outline on the difference between the Email services, and how to update your email for each.

Your IFTTT account email

You can head to ifttt.com or web, or tap the profile icon on mobile to access your account settings. Under the Email field, you will find the email address linked to your IFTTT account.

To update this email:

  • Erase the address currently listed there and enter your new email address.
  • Scroll down to the bottom of the page and press Update.
  • You will receive an email with a link to confirm the change.

The Gmail service

The Gmail service works specifically with Google accounts. It has two actions, send an email and send yourself an email.

To update this email:

  • Head to ifttt.com/gmail/settings, and click Reconnect on the account currently listed there.
  • Click sign in with google and add the desired account.
  • Click Allow.
Multi-account

If you are a Pro+ user, you can add multiple Google accounts. This will allow you to choose a specific Google account for each Applet. You can change the Google account used in an Applet by going to the Applet’s settings, clicking on the action, and selecting from the Gmail account dropdown.

The Email Service

The Email service is similar to Gmail, however it allows for any email address to be added. This service also includes Triggers, allowing you to send an email to trigger@applet.ifttt.com to trigger an Applet.

The Email service doesn’t support Multi-account, so you can only add one email connection at a time.

To update this email:

  • Head to ifttt.com/email/settings, and click reconnect.
  • Enter the email address you want to use.
  • Copy and paste the four digit code from your email to verify the account.
  • Any Applets using the Email service for a Trigger or Action will now be connected to the new account.

The Email Digest Service

The Email Digest service will send you a customized daily or weekly email full of information you’ve specified. You can create multiple Applets using Triggers for information-based services like RSS feed, Finance, Wall Street Journal, and link each one to an Email digest action. The Email Digest service will then compile all of this info into your daily or weekly email.

Like the Email service, Email Digest does not support Multi-account.

To update this email:

  • Head to ifttt.com/email_digest/settings, and click reconnect.
  • Enter the email address you want to use.
  • Copy and paste the four digit code from your email to verify the account.
  • Any Applets using Email Digest will now be connected to the new account.

Help! I can’t access my IFTTT account

The first thing you should do is reset your password. We’ll send a reset link to the email address associated with your IFTTT account.

If you’re unsure which email address you used to create your account, it’s possible you signed up using Apple’s Hide My Email feature. To check for this, follow the steps outlined in this guide.

It’s asking me for a 6 digit two-step verification code.

Enter in the two-step verification code from the authentication method you set up when you enabled the extra layer of security on your account. If you don’t have that method available, enter the backup code that you saved when you turned on two-step verification.

I’m not getting a two-step code or my backup code isn’t working.

There could be a few reasons for this:

  • The phone number you use for SMS two-step verification has changed
  • You’re located in a country where SMS two-step verification may not work reliably
  • You switched phones and no longer have the authentication app installed
  • The backup code may have a typo or the one you saved is an older one, backup codes reset each time you disable and re-enable two-step verification

Can you reset my phone number or authentication method?

To maintain the security of your account, IFTTT can’t update the phone number or your authentication method. We also don’t have access to your backup codes.

What are my options?

If you’re still stuck, the only option is to delete your IFTTT account so you can recreate it with the same email address. Please contact us with this request and we’ll take care of it for you.

This will delete all current connections on your account. This cannot be reversed.

Contact us

How do I delete my IFTTT account?

We’re sorry to see you go! If you’re sure you want to leave IFTTT, here’s how you can delete your account:

  • Sign in and navigate to your Account settings.

  • Scroll to the bottom of the page and click Delete my account.

  • Take a moment to tell us why you’re leaving, and enter your password. 💡 Note: If you signed up for IFTTT via Google or Facebook login, you would need to first reset your password with your email address attached to your Google or Facebook account, and then you can use the new password to delete your account.

  • Deleting your account cannot be undone. If you’re still sure you want to delete your account, select Delete my account on this screen. You can only delete your account via the IFTTT website. Deleting the IFTTT app on your phone will not delete your account.

How do I reset my password?

If you’ve lost your password or want to reset it, head to ifttt.com/forgot.

Enter the email address associated with your account, click Reset password, and we’ll send you an email with a link to reset your password.

How do I unsubscribe from IFTTT emails?

You may see emails from IFTTT in your inbox for a few different reasons, though they largely fall into a couple of categories.

Account emails

To manage your email subscriptions:

  • Sign in to your account settings

  • Go to Email communication

  • Uncheck the box next to the type of email you no longer wish to receive

  • Save your changes by clicking Update Here’s some more detail on what these emails are for:

  • Important alerts for your account - We strongly recommend keeping this checked. These alerts are sent out when action from you is necessary to fix an issue or if there’s an upcoming change to a service you use that may impact you. We’ll also email you if a service you use is being removed from IFTTT.

  • News, tips, and features - We send out newsletters periodically to make sure you don’t miss out on new services, helpful blog content, cool new collections, and more.

  • Promotions and discounts for products available on IFTTT - We work with hundreds of interesting partners. When one of them has a promotion or feature to share, we may email you if we think it’s relevant to your interests.

  • Join our beta - Opt-in to our beta communications if you’re interested in early access to new apps, features, and more.

  • Platform updates, announcements, and reports - Users who have access to the Developer Dashboard as part of a partner organization will get these emails. In addition to periodic platform newsletters, we may send you important feature announcements, new opportunities for your service, and account-specific insights.

Emails sent via an Applet

You may also receive emails from IFTTT in the form of an Applet that uses Email, Email Digest, Gmail, or another email service. You can’t unsubscribe from those by visiting account settings — instead, go to ifttt.com or the IFTTT app to find the Applet and turn it off. If you can’t figure out where the emails are coming from, check out these tips on how to find them and how to contact us if you need help.

How do I update my phone number?

Background

There are a few different ways you might be using your phone number with IFTTT. It could be used for two factor authentication, the IFTTT phone call service, or the IFTTT SMS service. This guide explains how to update the phone number used for each one. Please note that updating the number for one service will not change the number used for the others.

For SMS two-step verification

As of 2023, IFTTT no longer supports SMS for setting up new two step verification. If your account had SMS-based two step verification enabled before that date it will continue to work, but you will not be able to update the phone number used. Instead, you can remove two step verification and then turn it back on using an authenticator app. Follow the steps below:

  • Disable two-step verification by visiting your account settings page
  • Under Two-step verification, select Disable two-step verification
  • Enter your existing 6 digit verification code or 16 digit backup code, along with your password when prompted.
  • Once two step verification has been disabled, you can turn it back on using an authenticator app by selecting the Enable two step verification button.
  • Finish the setup process in your authentication app.
    • 💡Note: Remember to save your backup code! If you lose access to your authenticator app, you won’t be able to recover your account without it.
For Applets

Your phone number can be used with Applets that include a Trigger or Action from the Phone Call service or the SMS service. When you update the phone number for one of these services, all Applets that use that service will automatically use the new number. You will not need to make any changes to the Applets themselves.

If you need to update the number, follow these steps:

IFTTT SMS Service
  • Head to ifttt.com
  • Press the Reconnect button on the bottom of the page
  • Enter your phone number in the Phone Number field and check the boxes covering IFTTT’s terms and confirm you’d like to receive SMS messages from IFTTT, then press send PIN
  • Check your phone and enter the 4 digit PIN into the Pin field then press update
IFTTT Phone Call Service
  • Head to ifttt.com
  • Press the Reconnect button on the bottom of the page
  • Enter your phone number in the Phone Number field then press send PIN
  • You will receive an automated phone call with a 4 digit PIN. Enter that PIN in the Pin field and select Update.

How do I update my time zone?

Background

IFTTT accounts can have two different time zones, and they serve different purposes:

  • Your IFTTT account time zone controls how times are displayed in your Applet activity feed.
  • The Date & Time service time zone determines the time zone that Applets using Date & Time Triggers will use to run. For flexibility, your account time zone and the Date & Time service time zone can be set differently. This is useful if you’re temporarily in a new location and want your activity feed to show local times while still having your Applets trigger based on your original time zone. Updating one does not automatically change the other. See below for steps to update either or both time zones.

IFTTT account settings

Follow these steps to update your Account timezone which will affect timestamps shown in your activity feeds:

  • Sign in to your account settings
  • Under Time zone, select the right zone for your region using the drop down menu
  • Save your changes by scrolling to the bottom of the screen and selecting Update

The Date & Time service

Follow these steps to update your Date & Time service time zone, which will affect when Applets using Date & Time Triggers run:

  • Navigate to the Date & Time service
  • Select ⚙︎ Settings in the top right
  • Select Update time zone
  • Save your changes by pressing Connect

If a single timestamp seems incorrect

Sometimes an Applet’s activity feed looks correct overall, but one specific entry such as a Triggered At time appears at the wrong time. This can happen when the connected service itself is using a different time zone.

Example:

You live in Pacific Time, and your IFTTT account is set to Pacific. However, if you connect a thermostat that is set to Eastern Time in its own settings, using it as a Trigger may cause the activity feed to show the Triggered At time in Pacific, but three hours ahead. This happens because the thermostat reported the event in Eastern Time.

To resolve this:

Check the time zone settings for the connected service in its app or website and adjust them to match your local time.

Do I need to update for daylight savings time?

You do not need to manually adjust your IFTTT account or Date & Time service for daylight savings time. If the time zone you’ve selected observes daylight savings, the change will occur automatically and your Applets will continue to run at the correct local time.

How do I update my username or email address?

IFTTT account

To edit your account’s username or email address, follow the instructions below.

  • Sign in to your account and head to Account settings.
  • Click on the username or email address field and enter the username or email address you’d like to use.
  • Save your changes by scrolling down and clicking Update. If you change your email address, you’ll be sent a verification email. Be sure to open the new email and click the Confirm email change button to confirm your new email address.

Email Applets

Changing your IFTTT account email will not affect Applets using the Email or Email Digest service. To update those, you’ll have to visit Email settings or Email Digest settings, click Reconnect, and complete the verification process.

Updating your email address does not affect the title of your Email Applets. To change that, head to your Applet page, select the Applet you want to rename, and click Edit title.

How do I use two-step verification on my account?

Two-step verification adds an extra level of security to your account by requiring both your password and a verification code to sign in. It’s not required, but we do recommend enabling it if possible.

Enabling two-step verification

  • Sign in to your account settings.
  • Under two-step verification, click Enable two-step verification.
  • Enter your password.
  • Finish the setup process in your authentication app. Keep your backup code in a safe place! If you lose access to your authenticator app, this backup code will be necessary to access your IFTTT account. For account security reasons, we will be unable to retrieve your backup codes for you.

Disabling two-step verification

💡Note: You must have access to your existing authentication method or your backup code in order to turn off two-step verification. IFTTT cannot recover your account if you do not have one of those two.

  • Sign in to your IFTTT account settings.
  • Under two-step verification, click Disable two-step verification.
  • Enter your password.
  • Enter your two-step verification code or the backup code generated when you turned on two-step verification.

Once you’ve created an Applet through ifttt.com/create, it will appear on your My Applets page. If you want to temporarily disable the Applet, simply toggle the connected slider off when viewing the Applet. You can turn it back on later by pressing the slider again. Keep in mind, any Applets listed on your My Applets page will count toward the Applet limit for both free and Pro users. If you no longer plan to use an Applet, you can archive it to remove it from your active Applets list.

How to archive an Applet

An Applet can be archived by viewing the Applet, then pressing the Archive link at the bottom of the Applet:

Once archived the Applet will no longer run, and will not appear on your My Applets page.

The Applet will appear on your My Applets page. Keep in mind, restoring an Applet doesn’t automatically re-enable it. After restoring, go to ifttt.com/my_applets and re-enable the Applet by pressing the connect button.

Deleting an archived Applet

Applets that are archived won’t be deleted. If you’d prefer to permanently delete an Applet you can do so by following the steps in this guide.

I lost my two-step verification backup code. Can you reset it or issue a new one?

Signing in to IFTTT with Google, Facebook, or Apple

IFTTT offers a couple of different sign-in methods, called Social login, in addition to the standard email and password login. Current options include signing in with Apple, Google, and Facebook.

Can I still log in using my username and password?

Absolutely. New users signing in with Social login for the first time still have a password, it’s just randomly generated. The only time you may need to use it is if you’re connecting IFTTT to a service that doesn’t play well with Social login. You can update your password at any time by resetting it.

Can I still use two-step authentication with Social login?

Of course! If you have it set up, you’ll be prompted to enter your two-step verification code when you sign in.

I already have an IFTTT account. Can I use Social login?

You sure can, just make sure the email on your IFTTT account matches the one on your Google, Facebook, or Apple account. Otherwise, you may have an extra IFTTT account on your hands. If everything looks good, go to your account settings and scroll to Linked accounts to set up Social login.

Why do I still have to connect Google, Facebook, and iOS services separately?

Using Google, Facebook, or Apple to sign in to your IFTTT account doesn’t give IFTTT the same types of permissions needed for other services to function. You’ll still need to connect those individually.

Can I remove a Social login from my account?

Yes, to do so go to ifttt.com, scroll down to the Linked accounts heading, the click or tap unlink.

Why Did IFTTT Create My Account with a @privaterelay.appleid.com Email?

Apple’s Private Relay (Hide My Email) is a privacy feature that lets you create randomly generated email addresses when signing into an app or website. These addresses forward emails to the email linked to your Apple ID, helping keep your real email private. You can read more about Hide My Email on Apple’s help center.

Using Hide My Email When creating an IFTTT Account

When creating an IFTTT account, if you select Continue with Apple, it will create an IFTTT account using your Apple ID. As part of the signup process, you’ll see an option to enable Hide My Email.

Continue with Apple shown as an option when creating an IFTTT Account

If you don’t select Hide My Email, your IFTTT account will be created using the email address linked to your Apple ID.

If you do select Hide My Email, Apple will generate a private relay address to receive emails from IFTTT related to your account.

These private relay addresses use the domain @privaterelay.appleid.com and include a randomly generated string before the @ sign. This is the email address associated with your IFTTT account.

Accessing Your IFTTT Account Created Using Hide My Email

If you created your account using Hide My Email, just go to ifttt.com/login, select Continue with Apple, and sign in with your iCloud credentials if prompted.

Tap Continue with Apple to sign in using Apple Single Sign On

How to Find Your Private Relay Address for IFTTT

If you used Sign in with Apple when creating your IFTTT account, and you’re having trouble finding the email address linked to your account, here’s how you can locate it:

On iPhone:

  1. Open Settings and tap your name to access Apple ID settings.

  2. Tap Sign in with Apple.

  3. Find and tap the IFTTT app from the list.

  4. Look under Hide My Email for the private relay address Apple generated for IFTTT.

On Mac:

  1. Open System Settings, then go to Apple ID.

  2. Navigate to Password & Security > Apps Using Apple ID.

  3. Click IFTTT, then view the address listed under Hide My Email.

On Apple’s Website:

  1. Go to appleid.apple.com and sign in.

  2. Under Sign-In & Security, click Sign in with Apple.

  3. Find IFTTT, then view the email address used.

Can You Change the Login Email Linked to Your IFTTT Account?

Yes! If you’d prefer to use your real email instead of a private relay address, you can update your login email at any time by visiting this page in your account settings.

To do so, enter a new email address in the Email field then scroll down and press update.

Can You Use a Private Relay Address to Send Emails on IFTTT?

At the moment, Apple’s Private Relay addresses don’t allow sending emails through IFTTT’s Gmail or Email services. If you’re using either service, you’ll need to update the connected email to a regular address via the service connection page in your IFTTT account.

Seeing Duplicate Charges on IFTTT?

If you’re seeing duplicate charges, it’s possible you have more than one IFTTT account; one with your real email and another using your Private Relay address. Try logging in using the Continue with Apple option to check for a second account tied to a private relay email.

How do Active Devices work on Spotify Actions

The Spotify service on IFTTT supports eight actions that can be used to integrate Spotify with other triggers available on IFTTT. These actions range from managing playlists and saving tracks to starting and stopping a playback.

The actions currently supported by the Spotify service

Most of these actions work even if Spotify isn’t open or playing music. However, three actions do require an active Spotify device to function:

  • Start playback
  • Pause playback
  • Skip track These actions are designed to be used when a track is playing or paused on an active Spotify device. For example, you could create an Applet using the Ring service where if a New Ring is Detected pause the Spotify playback, then five minutes resume the playback using the Start playback action by linking two Applets together.

The above Applet would only run if there is an playback on an active device. If not the Applet will return an error stating “You must have an active device. None was found.”

Troubleshooting steps:

The Start playback, Pause playback, and Skip track action will run as long as these conditions are met:

  • Spotify must be open and actively running on your device when the applet is triggered.
    • If you’re using the Spotify Mobile app, the app will need to remain open on your device. For example, if the app is opened and a playback is started then closed and re-opened the Applet will no longer run
    • If you’re using a playback through Spotify.com the browser window will need to remain open
  • A track must be shown as being active as seen in the screen grab below
  • Your must have a Spotify Premium subscription Example of a paused playback on the Spotify mobile app

To ensure your Spotify action runs properly, follow these steps:

  • Open Spotify on your device.
  • Start playing a track, then manually pause it.
  • Leave Spotify open and do not close the app or switch to another playback device.
  • Make sure your phone is unlocked when the trigger is expected to run.

Additional considerations:

  • The other 5 actions available on Spotify as well as the Spotify service’s triggers and queries do not require an active device to run
  • Applets using a time based trigger with a Spotify action, such as every day at X time then Start playback can be used but the playback would need to be queued up and paused in advance of the Applet running.

How to Configure the OneDrive Folder Path for Microsoft 365 Excel Applets

Background

When creating Applets that use Microsoft 365 Excel, you will see a field labeled “Enter the folder path…”.

This field tells your Applet exactly where your file is located in OneDrive / Microsoft 365 Excel online.

The Applet relies on this path to either watch for changes when using Triggers or make updates when performing Actions. Entering the path correctly ensures that the Applet works with the correct file in your OneDrive.

How to format your response

Use the following guidelines to specify the path and avoid common formatting issues:

  • Folders should be separated by a /
  • Spaces in folder names are allowed (e.g., IFTTT/My Tweets)
  • Spaces in file names are also allowed
  • The “My Files” landing page in OneDrive (the home page section of OneDrive) is not considered a folder.
  • Leaving this field blank will direct the Applet to your My Files root directory.

Example 1: A folder on your homepage

If your folder is located at the top level of your OneDrive, simply enter the folder name in the Folder path field.

For example, if your top-level folder is named IFTTT, just enter IFTTT — no additional text is required.

To allow your Applet to monitor or update files within this folder, the field should be set as:

This will direct the Applet to the folder called IFTTT. This folder must be located on your OneDrive “My Files” homepage.

Example 2: No folder path

If your file is stored directly in your OneDrive root directory, meaning it appears in My Files and isn’t inside any folder like Example Spreadsheet shown here:

Then the Folder Path field can be left blank:

This setup tells the Applet to use your OneDrive My Files root directory. For Applets with an Action, it will update the file you’ve specified.

Example 3: Folder names with spaces and subfolders

In this example, the folder we’d like to monitor includes a space and is also a sub-folder of the “Work Places” folder, which is itself a sub-folder of the “IFTTT” folder:

Special characters are not required for spaces, so the folder name can be entered exactly as it appears in OneDrive. Additionally, folders can be separated by / to denote hierarchy:

This format will direct the Applet to the ‘IFTTT’ folder, then the ‘Work Places’ folder, and finally to the folder titled “Nov 20”.

Finding the folder path within OneDrive

If you are inside the OneDrive folder you’re trying to specify, you can find the folder path at the top of your screen in the breadcrumb navigation:

Example 1:

To specify this folder in the Applet, you would enter:

This tells the Applet exactly which folder to monitor or update within your OneDrive.

Configuring the “The Name of the Worksheet” Field

The “The Name of the Worksheet” field lets you choose which sheet in your workbook the action should run on. This is important because a workbook can have multiple sheets, and selecting the wrong one will prevent your applet from working correctly.

Example:

If your workbook has a sheet named Sheet1 and you want your applet to perform actions on it, the field should be filled exactly as:

  • Verify that the sheet exists in the workbook before running the applet.

How to Customize Your Facebook Lead Forms

If you’re a small business owner or an entrepreneur, the Facebook Lead Ad integration on IFTTT can help boost your business and attract more clients.

We’ll guide you through the step-by-step process of customizing your Facebook Leads forms in any language or creating custom questions.

How to map question fields for forms in any language

Your Facebook Lead Ads Instant Forms can be publicly displayed in any language you prefer.

If you have an Instant Form in any language other than English and want to include it in an IFTTT Applet, then there are a few quick and easy steps that you must follow in order for IFTTT to receive the form data as ingredients properly.

Note: Your form will still be shown in your preferred language. These steps only configure how Facebook sends the form data to IFTTT for optimal Applet performance.

  • Navigate to Instant Forms tool within your Meta Business Suite account
  • Create a new form or edit a draft form.
  • Under the Settings tab, select the desired language for your form under the Form Configuration section.
  • Under the Field Names section, edit each field name used in your form to match the following default English values. Edit for any of the following fields that appear in your form. Please note, this won’t affect the content tab so your fields will still appear in the language of your choice but will ensure that data is passed to the relevant IFTTT ingredients Form Field
